# isaxcheck fields-layer allowlist — the justified residual of
# `isaxcheck --layer=fields --classes=MBR`, which compares the InsnFields
# the dependency model records (what the trace is BUILT from) against
# LLVM MC.  Same file format and matching rules as isaxcheck_allow.txt;
# a separate file because the allowlist loader is layer-blind: an F row
# in the boundary file would go dead in every boundary run and fail that
# gate, exactly as W rows needed isaxcheck_fixups.txt.
#
# Author: Maccoy Merrell
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0-or-later
#
# WHY THESE ISAs.  This layer is the static-decode oracle the owner named
# for mipsel and aarch64, the two ISAs with no external ground truth (no
# PIN, no Spike): it is what must alert when the QEMU-execution-derived
# register capture of the behavioural-oracle arc disagrees with what the
# trace records today.  x86_64 and riscv64 close that loop through PIN and
# Spike as well, so the layer is a SECOND opinion there rather than the
# only one -- but a second opinion that is gated is worth more than one
# that is not, and the residual that kept riscv64 out is now triaged
# (#169, below).
#
# x86_64 IS NOW GATED TOO, and the history is worth keeping because it is
# how this file nearly acquired a false statement: an early attempt was
# killed by a 900 s timeout and reported "0 NEW rows", which is a
# TIMEOUT'S output and not a clean sweep.  The rows below come from a
# completed sweep of all 1,248,657,408 encodings, triaged into seven
# named families -- and the one number that must be read alongside them
# is `size_gap=`, the encodings the comparison declines to score because
# the two decoders read different instruction lengths.
#
# THE ROWS ARE THE OBSERVED RESIDUAL, EXACTLY.  Every row was produced by
# a full sweep (151.0 M mipsel / 163.6 M aarch64 encodings, Capstone
# 6.0.0-Alpha7, LLVM per the gate's summary line) and triaged into the
# family comments below; the D (decode-agreement / subtarget-gap) class is
# absent because the fields gate runs --classes=MBR — decode agreement is
# the boundary gate's property, gated there.  No wildcards: an exact row
# goes DEAD the moment either decoder or the dependency model moves, and
# the gate fails on dead rows, which is the tripwire that keeps this file
# maintained.  Do not edit a line to make the gate green.
#
# MOST OF THIS RESIDUAL IS THE BOUNDARY RESIDUAL SEEN ONE LAYER DOWN.
# The fields are the boundary plus the plugin's repairs, so where the
# plugin repairs nothing, a boundary disagreement passes straight
# through; those families cross-reference the boundary family in
# isaxcheck_allow.txt, whose comments carry the verification.  What is
# genuinely NEW at this layer is the plugin's own classification: the
# trap / MOPS / return branch taxonomy, prefetch-as-load memop modeling,
# and the system-register writes the tracer records that LLVM's
# instruction descriptions structurally cannot name.

# =====================================================================
# mipsel
# =====================================================================

# Control-flow the ISA table classifies and LLVM's per-opcode flags do
# not.  Traps and exception returns (break / syscall / sdbbp / eret /
# deret) are BRANCH_SYSCALL_TYPE-class on the fields side because the BB
# chain must seal a true BB at a trap — the same deliberate
# classification the fixups file asserts for the aarch64 trap space, and
# the same reason.  jalr.hb is a genuine jump-and-link (hazard-barrier
# form) whose LLVM description carries no branch flag at all.  The two
# `jr` rows are the operand-dependent cases per-opcode flags cannot
# express: `jr $ra` IS a return (fields say so, LLVM's JR cannot), and
# the jalr-with-rd=$zero alias links nothing (LLVM's JALR says call,
# fields correctly say plain indirect branch).  Boundary twin:
# `mipsel B-ret-mismatch jr cs-ret`.  Verified by --hex probes of
# 0c000000 (syscall) and 0800e003 (jr $ra), 2026-08-12.
mipsel FB-call-mismatch jr cs-nocall
mipsel FB-flow-presence break cs-yes llvm-no
mipsel FB-flow-presence deret cs-yes llvm-no
mipsel FB-flow-presence eret cs-yes llvm-no
mipsel FB-flow-presence jalr.hb cs-yes llvm-no
mipsel FB-flow-presence sdbbp cs-yes llvm-no
mipsel FB-flow-presence syscall cs-yes llvm-no
mipsel FB-ret-mismatch jr cs-ret

# `jalr rd, rs` WITH AN EXPLICIT rd: the fields-layer twin of the boundary
# row `mipsel R-wr-missing jalr +r#`, and it carries that row's whole
# derivation.  MIPS32 writes GPR[rd] and nothing else; LLVM's JALR
# MCInstrDesc carries `Defs = [RA]` for the one-operand spelling and
# Capstone's MIPS tables come from LLVM, so the pair is one source, refuted
# by the ISA manual and by QEMU's own `blink = rt`.  disas/capstone.c drops
# the phantom, gated on the operand list naming a link target other than
# $ra, so `jalr $t9` keeps its REG_LR write and `jalr $sp,$v0` does not.
# Removable with its boundary twin.
mipsel FR-wr-missing jalr +REG_LR

# LLVM's LUXC1 / SUXC1 descriptions carry neither mayLoad nor mayStore;
# the instructions are the unaligned-indexed FP load and store, and the
# fields side records exactly one load / one store slot for them.  LLVM
# description gap, same class as the boundary twins
# `mipsel M-cs-mem-llvm-none luxc1 / suxc1`.  Verified --hex=0500004c:
# llvm mayLoad=0 mayStore=0 on a load.
mipsel FM-cs-mem-llvm-none luxc1
mipsel FM-cs-mem-llvm-none suxc1

# `pref` / `prefe` name a MEM operand whose access Capstone reports as
# NEITHER read nor write, and the boundary now infers READ from the opcode
# class (see the pref block in disas/capstone.c).  LLVM's descriptions carry
# mayLoad=0 for both, so the fields side declares a load lane and LLVM
# declares nothing -- the same LLVM description gap as luxc1/suxc1 above.
# x86_64 `prefetcht1` and aarch64 `prfm` both declare loads=1 and always did;
# mipsel was the only ISA of the three whose prefetch claimed no memory, and
# its runtime memop arrived ORPHANED because of it.
mipsel FM-cs-mem-llvm-none pref
mipsel FM-cs-mem-llvm-none prefe

# `cache` / `cachee` / `synci` are the same shape one step further out: a MEM
# operand Capstone reports with NEITHER access bit, an address the guest
# computed, and no data.  The fields side now declares the load lane the
# synthetic-EA record fills, so the template stops claiming loads=0 for an
# instruction whose entries carry a memop.  LLVM reports mayLoad=0 mayStore=0
# for all three -- which for `synci` is also the evidence that binutils'
# INSN_STORE_MEMORY is the outlier; QEMU emits nothing for it either
# (target/mips/tcg/translate.c:14403).  Boundary twins: the
# `mipsel M-cs-mem-llvm-none cache*` / `synci` rows.
mipsel FM-cs-mem-llvm-none cache
mipsel FM-cs-mem-llvm-none cachee
mipsel FM-cs-mem-llvm-none synci

# mayLoad / mayStore are LLVM SCHEDULING properties, not semantics, and
# LLVM sets both across the MIPS DSP-ASE arithmetic space to pin
# instruction order around DSPControl.  None of these instructions
# touches memory and the fields side reserves no memop slot for any of
# them.  Identical artifact and identical row set to the boundary's
# `mipsel M-llvm-mem-cs-none` block.  Verified --hex=521a007c
# (absq_s.ph): llvm mayLoad=1 mayStore=1 on a register-only op.
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none absq_s.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none absq_s.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none absq_s.w load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none addq_s.w load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none addsc load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none addu.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none addu_s.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none addwc load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgdu.eq.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgdu.le.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgdu.lt.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgu.eq.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgu.le.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpgu.lt.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none cmpu.eq.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none insv load
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none mul.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none mul_s.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none muleq_s.w.phl load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none muleq_s.w.phr load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none muleu_s.ph.qbl load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none muleu_s.ph.qbr load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none mulq_rs.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none mulq_rs.w load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none mulq_s.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none mulq_s.w load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none pick.ph load
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none pick.qb load
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none precr.qb.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none precrq_rs.ph.w load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none precrqu_s.qb.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none rddsp load
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none shll_s.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none shll_s.w load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none shllv.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none shllv.qb load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none shllv_s.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none shllv_s.w load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none subq_s.w load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none subu.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none subu_s.ph load store
mipsel FM-llvm-mem-cs-none wrdsp load store

# LWL / LWR read their own destination: the register keeps its unwritten
# bytes, so the old value is a true source.  The boundary's partial-merge
# workaround supplies the read (see the lwl/lwr entries in
# isaxcheck_allow.txt and the ambiguity-probe arc that added it); LLVM
# models the merge as a tied operand only on its store side, so its read
# set stays empty here.  The fields side is the CORRECT one.  Verified
# --hex=00000188: fields SRC{REG_GPR1,...} on lwl $at.
mipsel FR-rd-phantom lwl +REG_FP_REG
mipsel FR-rd-phantom lwl +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-rd-phantom lwl +REG_LR
mipsel FR-rd-phantom lwl +REG_SP
mipsel FR-rd-phantom lwr +REG_FP_REG
mipsel FR-rd-phantom lwr +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-rd-phantom lwr +REG_LR
mipsel FR-rd-phantom lwr +REG_SP

# DSPControl, which the tracer folds onto REG_FLAGS: bposge32 branches
# on its pos field (read), rddsp copies fields out of it (read), wrdsp
# writes fields into it (write), and mthlip both reads and increments
# pos (the read is the row; the write both sides agree on).  LLVM models
# no DSPControl use for any of them beyond mthlip's def.  The fields
# side is correct throughout.  Boundary twins: the `+dsp` rows.
mipsel FR-rd-phantom bposge32 +REG_FLAGS
mipsel FR-rd-phantom mthlip +REG_FLAGS
mipsel FR-rd-phantom rddsp +REG_FLAGS
mipsel FR-wr-phantom wrdsp +REG_FLAGS

# LLVM gives the FP condition branches (bc1t / bc1f and the -likely
# forms) an implicit def of $at, modelling its own long-branch assembler
# macro expansion rather than the architecture; the instruction writes
# no GPR.  Keeping the def would fabricate a WAW hazard on $at across
# every FP branch.  Boundary twin family: `mipsel R-wr-missing bc1* +r#`
# (whose comment carries the full story).  Verified --hex=00000145:
# llvm WR{r1} on bc1t.
mipsel FR-wr-missing bc1f +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bc1fl +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bc1t +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bc1tl +REG_GPR#

# CTCMSA — copy TO an MSA control register — is one disagreement seen
# from two sides: LLVM's MCInstrDesc carries the control register as a
# USE (so it sits in LLVM's read set, mapped through the register table
# through the register table), while Capstone and the tracer make it the
# DEF a copy-to-control-register instruction plainly writes.  Capstone
# and the tracer are correct.  Two id pairs appear because Capstone
# prints an MSA control register as a bare NUMBER, so the normaliser
# cannot tell control register N from CP0 register N and the read side
# resolves through whichever the tracer classified -- the split CP0 file
# now gives those two different classes.
# Boundary twins: `mipsel R-rd-missing ctcmsa +cop#` /
# `R-wr-phantom ctcmsa +cop#`, same single difference in the boundary
# vocabulary.
mipsel FR-rd-missing ctcmsa +REG_SYSMMU
mipsel FR-rd-missing ctcmsa +REG_COPROC0
mipsel FR-wr-phantom ctcmsa +REG_SYS
mipsel FR-wr-phantom ctcmsa +REG_FCSR
mipsel FR-wr-phantom ctcmsa +REG_SYSID

# Signatures the register-space rework surfaced under a new generic
# id.  Same residual, new name.
#
# THE IMPLICIT-REGISTER TABLE (disas/capstone.c, cap_x86_implicit) MOVED
# THIRTEEN RULES IN THIS FILE FROM LIVE TO DEAD, AND THEY ARE DELETED
# RATHER THAN KEPT.  Every one of them said "the tracer misses a register
# LLVM names" for an instruction whose whole dependency is implicit --
# xlatb, cmpxchg8b/16b, clzero, monitorx, mwaitx.  Capstone reports no
# implicit operand for them; the table supplies the architectural set, so
# the signature cannot occur any more and a rule that cannot fire is a
# false statement about the tracer.
#
# THE ROWS BELOW ARE WHAT THE REPAIR LEFT, in three classes, and none of
# them is a register the tracer dropped:
#
#  *-phantom  fs / gs / cr0 on SMSW, LMSW, SWAPGS, RD/WRFSBASE,
#             RD/WRGSBASE, LFS, LGS: the tracer names the control or
#             segment register and LLVM MC names NOTHING.  XED -- this
#             arc's primary reference -- names exactly what the tracer
#             now names, on the same encodings:
#               0f01e0    SMSW      XED src CR0        LLVM src -
#               0f01f0    LMSW      XED dst CR0        LLVM dst -
#               0f01f8    SWAPGS    XED GSBASE/GSBASE  LLVM -/-
#               f30faec0  RDFSBASE  XED src FSBASE     LLVM src -
#               f30faed0  WRFSBASE  XED dst FSBASE     LLVM dst -
#               0fb400    LFS       XED dst FS,RAX     LLVM dst RAX
#             LLVM MC models these as opaque; QEMU's translation of each
#             reads or writes precisely that state.  Recording MORE than
#             LLVM is the direction this project accepts.
#
#  mwaitx     the tracer writes RAX where LLVM writes nothing.  XED
#             writes RAX (the MONITORX/MWAITX extension returns its
#             status there).  The RBX read the tracer also adds is NOT
#             in this list because it made the tracer AGREE with LLVM.
#
#  *-missing  cs / ds on cmpxchg8b, cmpxchg16b and ud1 with a segment
#             override.  This is the SAME pre-existing class already
#             allowed here for cmpxchgb, cmpw and the rest -- the tracer
#             does not attribute the segment register of an explicit
#             memory operand -- and these three mnemonics only join it
#             now because the register half of their disagreement was
#             repaired and stopped masking the segment half.  The rule
#             they replace said "+REG_GPR#,REG_SEG0"; the narrower rule
#             is the honest one.
#
#             MONITORX / MWAITX with a 66, F2 or F3 prefix are the two
#             rows where the tracer now records LESS than LLVM MC, and
#             that is deliberate: XED REFUSES every prefixed form of both
#             (660f01fa, 660f01fb, f20f01fa, f20f01fb, f30f01fb all
#             decode to nothing) and reads f30f01fa as MCOMMIT, while
#             LLVM ignores the prefix and repeats the bare instruction.
#             The implicit-register table follows XED and declines to
#             attach MONITORX's operands to bytes XED says are not
#             MONITORX.
#
#  ud1        Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7 decoded UD1 (0F B9 /r) as two bytes
#             with no operands, which the same repair fixes; the row now
#             enters the comparison at all, and LLVM's own view of it
#             carries a memory operand for the register form and no
#             control-flow group.  Both differences are LLVM's.
#
x86_64 FR-wr-missing cltd +REG_GPR#
x86_64 FR-wr-missing cqto +REG_GPR#
x86_64 FR-wr-missing cwtd +REG_GPR#

# =====================================================================
# aarch64
# =====================================================================

# The exception-generation space — svc / hvc / smc / brk / hlt / udf /
# dcps<n> — classified BRANCH_SYSCALL_TYPE by the ISA table so the BB
# chain seals a true BB at the trap; none of them carries a control-flow
# flag in LLVM.  SVC is the load-bearing member: every system call in an
# AArch64 trace is one.  DRPS is the deliberate reversal — LLVM calls
# the debug-state exception return a branch and the table does not,
# because it is not an edge any consumer can follow.  This is the same
# classification isaxcheck_fixups.txt asserts (its aarch64 trap section
# carries the full story); boundary twins:
# `aarch64 B-flow-presence hvc / smc / svc`.
aarch64 FB-flow-presence brk cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence dcps1 cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence dcps2 cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence dcps3 cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence drps cs-no llvm-yes
aarch64 FB-flow-presence hlt cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence hvc cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence smc cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence svc cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence udf cs-yes llvm-no

# FEAT_MOPS bulk copy / set (cpyp / cpym / cpye / cpyfp / cpyfm / cpyfe
# and the set* family, times their read/write x temporal option
# suffixes).  BRANCH_REP is the tracer's own wire model, not an
# architectural claim: these are the ISA's only instructions whose
# memory fan-out is bounded by nothing but a register, so the ISA table
# hands them to the same self-loop fan-out the x86 REP-prefixed string
# ops use — the instruction becomes its own true BB and each execution
# emits one body entry per memory access (see the FEAT_MOPS comment in
# champsim_tracer_mnemonic_audit.py).  LLVM correctly reports no branch;
# the disagreement IS the design.  Verified --hex=2204801d (cpye):
# fields BRANCH_REP cond, register/flags dataflow agreeing with LLVM.
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpye cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyen cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyern cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyert cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyertn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyertrn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyertwn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyet cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyetn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyetrn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyetwn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyewn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyewt cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyewtn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyewtrn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyewtwn cs-yes llvm-no
aarch64 FB-flow-presence cpyfe cs-yes llvm-no

# The AArch64 FP control and status word, REG_FCSR, in both directions.
#
# LLVM MC models neither half: it reports no FPCR read on any FP
# instruction and has no notion of FPSR at all.  The tracer records both,
# from cap_aarch64_fp_status_contract() in disas/capstone.c, adjudicated
# against the Arm MRA ISA_A64_xml_A_profile-2022-12 execute pseudocode
# with QEMU's translator as the second reading.  FPCR is the FP
# datapath's INPUT -- rounding mode, FZ, DN, FEAT_AFP's AH/NEP/FIZ,
# FEAT_EBF16's EBF; FPSR is its OUTPUT -- the cumulative exception bits
# IXC/IOC/OFC/UFC/IDC and the Advanced SIMD saturation bit QC.  The
# generic register space folds both onto REG_FCSR.
#
# FR-rd-phantom rows are the FPCR read, FR-wr-phantom rows the FPSR
# write.  The read set excludes the bit movers (fabs/fneg/fcsel/fdup/
# fexpa/ftssel in their vector and SVE forms) and the write set
# additionally excludes the BF16 dot-product and matrix family and the
# SME array accumulate forms, so a row appearing in one list and not the
# other is the contract working, not an omission.
#
# FMOV IS IN NEITHER LIST, ON ANY FORM, which is where it differs from
# the other two bit movers that have scalar rows.  FABS and FNEG read
# FPCR.NEP through the merging door; FMOV does not go through it.  QEMU
# says so in its dispatch -- do_fp1_scalar_int_2fn (merging=true) for
# FABS/FNEG against `TRANS(FMOV_s, do_fp1_scalar_int, a, &f_scalar_fmov,
# false)` -- and the commit that added the parameter gives that as the
# reason it was needed (64339259a9).  The row this replaces was
# `FR-rd-phantom fmov +REG_FCSR,REG_SYSFPEN`; it covered FMOV (general),
# `fmov s0, w0`, which is a bit copy between register files with no
# conversion and therefore no rounding mode to consult.
#
# The FR-rd-missing rows are the other direction -- registers LLVM names
# and the tracer, having adjudicated them away, does not:
#   sqdmulh / sqrdmulh +REG_FCSR   integer saturating arithmetic reads no
#                                  control word (sqadd_advsimd.xml)
#   eretaa / eretab   +REG_LR      they authenticate ELR_ELx; x30 is not
#                                  touched (eretaa.xml)
#   mov / zero        +REG_MATRIX  SME ZERO and unpredicated MOVA write
#                                  the array without reading it
#                                  (zero_za1_ri.xml, mova_za2_z.xml)
#
# (mrs / mrrs / msr / msrr rows live in the system-register sections
# below, not here, whichever register they name.)
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom fabs +REG_FPCW,REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom fadda +REG_FPCW,REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom fccmp +REG_FPCW,REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom fccmpe +REG_FPCW,REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom fneg +REG_FPCW,REG_SYSFPEN
# THE ALWAYS-CONDITIONS.  LLVM MC gives every B.cond / BC.cond form the same
# condition-code Use, so `bc.al` and `bc.nv` arrive with `llvmRD{nzcv}` --
# and at cond<3:1> == '111' the architecture reads no flag at all.
# ARM DDI 0487C.a, shared/functions/system/ConditionHolds (J1-6366):
#
#     case cond<3:1> of
#         ...
#         when '111' result = TRUE;                 // AL
#     // Condition flag values in the set '111x' indicate always true
#     if cond<0> == '1' && cond != '1111' then result = !result;
#
# The '111' arm names no PSTATE field and the inversion below it is
# explicitly excluded for '1111', so NZCV is not an input to either
# encoding.  QEMU draws the same line -- trans_B_cond() emits a plain
# gen_goto_tb() with no arm_gen_test_cc() for 0xe and 0xf -- and
# disas/capstone.c's cap_aarch64_always_cond_contract() drops the read the
# reference mints, which is what makes this an `rd-missing` row rather than
# a silent agreement.  Removable if LLVM ever stops attaching the flags to
# the always-conditions; `cstool -d aarch64 1f000054` shows Capstone
# carrying the same claim, so the correction is not version-specific.
aarch64 FR-rd-missing bc.* +REG_FLAGS
aarch64 FR-rd-missing eretaa +REG_LR
aarch64 FR-rd-missing eretab +REG_LR
aarch64 FR-rd-missing mov +REG_MATRIX
aarch64 FR-rd-missing sqdmulh +REG_FPCW
aarch64 FR-rd-missing sqrdmulh +REG_FPCW
aarch64 FR-rd-missing zero +REG_MATRIX

# The system-register space reached through MRS / MRRS / MSR / MSRR.
# LLVM's descriptions carry the system register as an IMMEDIATE — the
# 15-bit encoding, unexpanded — so no register the tracer records for
# these instructions can EVER appear on LLVM's side: the read the tracer
# takes for MRS (REG_SYS / REG_FCSR / REG_TLS / REG_FLAGS by selector)
# is a phantom here, and the write it takes for MSR is missing-in-LLVM,
# structurally.  This is the documented Zicsr shape from the isaxcheck.cc
# header; what actually vouches for these dependencies is
# implicit_audit.py, not this gate.  REG_TLS is tpidr_el0 — every
# thread-local access begins there, the member with real dynamic
# weight.  REG_FLAGS via `msr nzcv` folds onto the same id ordinary
# flag-writing arithmetic uses, which is what makes `msr nzcv, x0`
# define what a later b.eq tests.  Boundary twins: the mrs/msr
# `+sys/+fcsr/+tls/+flags` blocks.
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_FCSR
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYS
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_FCSR
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_FLAGS
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYS
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_TLS
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_FCSR
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_FLAGS
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SYS
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_TLS
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_FCSR
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_SYS

# REG_SYSID is the same family with a second selector: the read-only
# implementation constants 1b6c4995a5 split out of REG_SYS (MIDR_EL1,
# MPIDR_EL1, REVIDR_EL1, CTR_EL0, DCZID_EL0, the ID_AA64* block).  That
# commit minted the id and taught disas/capstone.c to produce it without
# adding a rule on either layer, so these four arrived unallowed and are
# the whole of the aarch64 gate's residual.  The justification is the one
# written above and not a new one -- LLVM carries the system register as
# an immediate, so no register the tracer records here can appear on its
# side, whichever class the selector lands in.
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSID
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSID
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SYSID
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_SYSID

# LLVM gives the whole MSR-immediate-to-PSTATE form (and MRS) an NZCV
# def because one PSTATE field it can name IS NZCV; every alias inherits
# it, including the SME streaming-mode switches, which write PSTATE.SM /
# PSTATE.ZA and leave the condition flags alone.  For those members the
# def is LLVM's phantom and the fields side is right.  ONE member is the
# other way around: `mrs xN, RNDR` / `RNDRRS` really does set NZCV
# (Z flags entropy failure) and Capstone reports no flags write, so for
# that alias the missing write is REAL — a Capstone gap the boundary
# comment now names, carried by this same signature because keys collapse
# to the mnemonic.  Work item filed; the execution-derived capture of the
# behavioural-oracle arc closes it, and when it does this row goes DEAD
# and the gate demands its removal.  Verified --hex=02243bd5 (mrs x2,
# RNDR): llvm WR{nzcv,r2}, boundary and fields WR{r2} only.
aarch64 FR-wr-missing mrs +REG_FLAGS
aarch64 FR-wr-missing msr +REG_FLAGS
aarch64 FR-wr-missing smstart +REG_FLAGS
aarch64 FR-wr-missing smstop +REG_FLAGS

# MSR (immediate) -- the fields-layer twin of the boundary carve-out in
# isaxcheck_allow.txt.  The PSTATE field an MSR (immediate) writes IS an
# addressable system register, so the boundary names it and the dependency
# model records it: DAIF for daifset/daifclr (a read-modify-write, hence
# REG_SYS on both sides), SVCR for smstart/smstop, plus the SME enable gate
# those two read through CheckSMEAccess.  LLVM MC has no model for any of
# it -- Uses empty, Defs `nzcv`, which the four lines above already record
# as wrong -- so the whole difference is state the tracer carries and the
# reference does not.  See cap_aarch64_pstate_field and
# cap_aarch64_msr_imm_contract for the direction of each field.
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom msr +REG_SYS
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom smstart +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom smstop +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom smstart +REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom smstop +REG_VCTRL

# SME ZA, which LLVM models as immediates rather than registers where
# the tracer records REG_MATRIX: `zero {za1.s}` names its tile list as
# an immediate mask on LLVM's side (boundary twins: the `zero +za`
# rows).
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom zero +REG_MATRIX,REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom zero +REG_MATRIX

# mayLoad / mayStore as LLVM SCHEDULING properties again (see the
# mipsel DSP block and the boundary's aarch64 barrier/hint comment): the
# barrier and hint space, WFE/WFI/yield, and the SVE/SME members whose
# ordered state (FFR, ZA) LLVM pins by claiming memory.  None of them
# touches memory; the fields side reserves no memop slot.  Identical row
# set to the boundary's `aarch64 M-llvm-mem-cs-none` block.
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none bti load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none clrbhb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none clrex load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none csdb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none dfb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none dgh load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none dmb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none dsb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none esb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none gcsb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none hint load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none isb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none luti2 load
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none luti4 load
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none mov load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none nop load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none psb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none pssbb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none setffr store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none sev load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none sevl load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none ssbb load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none tcancel load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none tcommit load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none tstart load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none wfe load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none wfi load store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none wrffr store
aarch64 FM-llvm-mem-cs-none yield load store

# Prefetches: the fields side deliberately carries the address of
# PRFM / PRFUM / RPRFM as a load-class memop (GEN_OP_PREFETCH) — a
# prefetch with no address would be useless to a cache model — while
# LLVM's descriptions say no memory, which is architecturally true (a
# hint) and useless on the wire.  The disagreement is the tracer's
# design.  Verified --hex=220000d8 (prfm): fields loads=1, llvm
# mayLoad=0.
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none prfm
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none prfum
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none rprfm

# The SVE prefetches (PRFB / PRFD / PRFH / PRFW) are the same design decision
# and used to sit under `FM-llvm-mem-cs-none ... load store` -- LLVM claiming
# both directions, the fields side claiming neither.  The fields side now
# claims the load, because it always published one: the synthetic-EA callback
# mints an address-only memop for every one of these forms and the template
# had been declaring loads=0 underneath it.  What is left of the disagreement
# is LLVM's mayStore, which is a SCHEDULING property on a hint that stores
# nothing; the four `FM-llvm-mem-cs-none` rows are gone and the dead-rule
# detector is what would have said so.  Verified --hex=22c08085 (prfd):
# fields loads=1 stores=0, llvm mayLoad=1 mayStore=1.
aarch64 FM-miss-store prfb
aarch64 FM-miss-store prfd
aarch64 FM-miss-store prfh
aarch64 FM-miss-store prfw

# LLVM description gaps in the other direction: instructions that DO
# access memory with neither mayLoad nor mayStore set — DC ZVA (an
# architectural store of zeros), the LRCPC2 unscaled-offset loads
# (ldapursb/sh/sw), the FEAT_LS64 64-byte forms and the MTE bulk-tag
# stores (stgm / stzgm).  The fields side records the slots.  Boundary
# twins: the `aarch64 M-cs-mem-llvm-none` rows for the same mnemonics.
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none dc
# `ic ivau` joins `dc` for the same reason: cache maintenance BY VIRTUAL
# ADDRESS now carries the line it acts on.  GCSSTR / GCSSTTR join the
# store side: Capstone models no memory operand for them at all, LLVM names
# none either, and the Arm MRA resolves the row to mw=1.
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none ic
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none gcsstr
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none gcssttr
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none ld64b
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none ldapursb
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none ldapursh
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none ldapursw
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none st64b
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none st64bv
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none st64bv0
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none stgm
aarch64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none stzgm

# SME ZA-slice stores (st1b/h/w/d/q {zaNh...}): LLVM marks them mayLoad
# as well as mayStore — modelling the ZA tile read as memory — but no
# architectural load exists and the fields side reserves store slots
# only.  Boundary twins: `aarch64 M-miss-load st1b..st1w`.
aarch64 FM-miss-load st1b
aarch64 FM-miss-load st1d
aarch64 FM-miss-load st1h
aarch64 FM-miss-load st1q
aarch64 FM-miss-load st1w

# THE AArch64 LOAD-EXCLUSIVES, and the reason they only became visible at
# 733a65dfb0.  LLVM sets mayStore on ldxr / ldaxr / ldxp / ldaxp (with the
# byte and halfword forms) the same way it sets it on the barriers above:
# the exclusive monitor is unmodelled state, and mayStore is how the
# scheduler is told not to move memory across it.  Architecturally a
# load-exclusive reads memory and marks the monitor; it writes nothing.
#
# Until 733a65dfb0 nothing appeared here, and that was two errors
# cancelling rather than agreement: Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7 reports
# READ|WRITE on the MEM operand of these forms, so the boundary claimed a
# store too and the comparison matched.  The Capstone side is now
# corrected (cap_aarch64_exclusive_mem_access), and the disagreement it
# leaves is LLVM's alone.
#
# Which side is right was MEASURED, not argued: on an aarch64 probe the
# ldaxr entry declared `st[%gp1]` with no address and every execution
# delivered only `ld=`, never `st=` -- a slot QEMU's memop callbacks can
# never fill.  ldar is the control (READ only, unchanged) and the LSE
# read-modify-writes (ldadd, swp) keep both slots, so this is specific to
# the exclusives rather than a blanket rule about acquire/release.
# Evidence: /mnt/md0/QEMU/cst_runs/p3/t177/FINDING.md.  Boundary twins:
# `aarch64 M-miss-store ldxr..ldaxp`, same rows one layer down.
aarch64 FM-miss-store ldaxp
aarch64 FM-miss-store ldaxr
aarch64 FM-miss-store ldaxrb
aarch64 FM-miss-store ldaxrh
aarch64 FM-miss-store ldxp
aarch64 FM-miss-store ldxr
aarch64 FM-miss-store ldxrb
aarch64 FM-miss-store ldxrh

# REAL DEPENDENCY-MODEL GAPS, inherited from Capstone operand reporting
# and allowlisted at the boundary — named here as defects, not vouched
# for.  These are exactly the rows the behavioural-oracle arc's
# execution-derived register capture exists to close; each goes DEAD
# when that lands, and the gate then demands its removal.
#
#   - FEAT_LS64: LD64B writes and ST64B/ST64BV/ST64BV0 read a GROUP of
#     eight consecutive registers; Capstone names only the first, so the
#     fields record one of eight.  Verified --hex=22903ff8: llvm
#     RD{r1..r9}, fields RD{r1,r2}.  Zero dynamic weight in current
#     workloads (no guest uses FEAT_LS64), which is why it is tolerable
#     to carry, not why it is right.
#   - PSEL's vector-select index read (the W register in p1.b[w12, 0])
#     is absent from Capstone's detail.  Boundary twin:
#     `aarch64 R-rd-missing psel +r#`.

# LDG marks its address base register written in Capstone's detail; the
# instruction has no writeback and only Xt is written.  Capstone
# access-flag defect, inherited; fabricates a WAW on the base register
# wherever MTE LDG appears (kernel MTE paths only today).  Boundary
# twins: `aarch64 R-wr-phantom ldg +r# / +sp`, same defect one layer up.
# Verified --hex=224060d9: boundary WR{r1,r2}, llvm WR{r2}.
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom ldg +REG_FP_REG
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom ldg +REG_GPR#
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom ldg +REG_LR
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom ldg +REG_SP

# The TLBI operations that take no Xt argument are encoded in the SYS
# format whose Rt the architecture requires to be 0b11111; LLVM keeps
# the syntactic operand and reports it read whatever the field holds, so
# only reserved encodings no assembler emits (Rt != 31) disagree.
# Capstone and the fields are right.  Boundary twin:
# `aarch64 R-rd-missing tlbi +r#`, whose comment is the verification.
aarch64 FR-rd-missing tlbi +REG_GPR#

# =====================================================================
# riscv64
# =====================================================================
#
# TRIAGED 2026-08-22 (#169, discharging the #95 residual).  Every row
# below was produced by a full `--layer=fields --classes=MBR` sweep and
# is the observed residual EXACTLY -- 348 rows, 253 mnemonics, six
# signature classes.  The through-line: in every class measured the
# fields side is the one telling the truth and LLVM MC is the one that
# cannot express it.  That is why these are allowlisted rather than
# fixed -- there is nothing on our side to fix.
#

# Traps as control flow.  ebreak / ecall / unimp end a basic block on the
# fields side -- the same classification the mipsel break / syscall rows
# above carry -- while LLVM's per-opcode flags do not mark them.
#   3 rows
riscv64 FB-flow-presence ebreak cs-yes llvm-no
riscv64 FB-flow-presence ecall cs-yes llvm-no
riscv64 FB-flow-presence unimp cs-yes llvm-no

# `jr 1(ra)` is not a return: a jump through ra with a NON-ZERO offset
# does not resume the caller, and the fields side declines to call it
# one.
#   1 rows
riscv64 FB-ret-mismatch jr cs-noret

# `vsetivli` -- THE SAME CLASS, ON THE FAMILY THE GENERATED BLOCK AT THE FOOT
# OF THIS FILE DOES NOT OWN.
#
# `vsetivli` takes its AVL and its vtype as IMMEDIATES, so the vector CSRs it
# reads have no register operand for LLVM to name -- the class immediately
# above, on the vector-configuration pair rather than on fcsr.  Sail states
# the read; the deletion that took it out was landed and REVERTED when Sail
# contradicted it (5ae4742f99).
#
# IT LIVES HERE AND NOT WITH THE `SR-rd-*` ROWS AT THE FOOT, and that is the
# point of moving it (FINDING 77-B).  It was appended INSIDE the generated
# block, whose own header said a full regeneration was owed, so the first
# regeneration would have deleted it.  The generator owns `SR-rd-*` and
# nothing else.
#   1 row
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom vsetivli +REG_VCTRL

# LLVM MC does not model a CSR as a REGISTER.  In its instruction
# descriptions the CSR is an immediate operand, so for every csr* form
# llvmRD and llvmWR are BOTH empty and the fields side is the only one
# naming the state the instruction touches.  `csrc fflags, zero`
# (73301000) reads fflags to clear bits in it; the fields side records
# REG_FCSR, LLVM records nothing at all.  The FP arithmetic entries are
# the same fact from the other side: they read the rounding mode out of
# fcsr, which LLVM's operand list has no way to say.  The tracer is
# right here and the oracle is silent, not contradicting.
#   85 rows
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrc +REG_FCSR

# THE CSRs THAT DECIDE WHETHER AN INSTRUCTION TRAPS.  LLVM MC has no
# legality axis at all -- it describes the operands an encoding names,
# and a trap gate is named by nothing -- so every one of these reads is
# a phantom to it.  They are in the fields set under R7.4: a pending
# write to the gating CSR must resolve before the instruction may
# proceed, which is an edge a renaming regfile has to respect.
#
#   wfi           mstatus.TW picks between executing, an Illegal
#                 Instruction and a Virtual Instruction trap
#   cbo.*         menvcfg / senvcfg / henvcfg CBCFE, CBIE and CBZE gate
#                 the cache-block operations per privilege
#   vector FP     the unit has no rm field, so fcsr.frm is always the
#                 rounding mode and a reserved frm makes the instruction
#                 illegal.  Only the members that ROUND NOTHING appear
#                 here: the rest already carried REG_FCSR through the
#                 fflags accumulate, so LLVM's silence was already
#                 covered by an existing row.
#
# vfmerge.vfm carries two phantoms at once and so has two rows: the frm
# gate, and the mask-register group LLVM names only the base of (it was
# in the register-group block above until the gate read joined it).
# sspush / sspopchk / ssrdp are NOT here -- LLVM cannot decode Zicfiss
# at all, so their rows sit in the #169 undecodable-extension residual.
#   17 rows

# --- R7.4, continued: the privilege gate and the flush scope of a TLB
#     maintenance or hypervisor load/store.  Sail decides all three in the
#     clause head:
#       sfence.vma / sinval.vma   Illegal at U; at S when mstatus.TVM, at VS
#                                 when hstatus.VTVM (base_insts.sail:725-737).
#                                 The VMID that scopes the flush comes from
#                                 hgatp (get_vmid).
#       hfence.gvma / hinval.gvma mstatus.TVM (hext_insts.sail:183-189)
#       hfence.vvma / hinval.vvma hgatp.VMID, the flush scope
#       hlv / hlvx / hsv          hstatus.HU decides whether U may execute
#                                 them, hstatus.SPVP selects the effective
#                                 privilege the access runs at
#     Zicbop's `prefetch.*` is deliberately NOT here: every arm of its
#     translation is a no-op, so it cannot trap and its result depends on no
#     CSR -- the mstatus its Sail clause names is read only by the
#     translation of its own operand, which is out of scope on both sides.
#     LLVM's instruction descriptions carry no implicit CSR access at all, so
#     none of these can appear on its side.
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hfence.gvma +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hfence.vvma +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hinval.gvma +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hinval.vvma +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hlv.b +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hlv.bu +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hlv.d +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hlv.h +REG_SYS

riscv64 FR-rd-phantom hlv.wu +REG_SYS

# RISC-V HINT encodings, where the fields side records NO dataflow and
# LLVM reports the underlying encoding's register fields.  The spec makes
# these hints, not operations: C.ADDI with imm=0 and rd!=x0 is a HINT (a
# C.NOP is the rd=x0 case), Zimop's cmop.N and Zihintntl's ntl.* / c.ntl.*
# are hints by construction, and c.slli64 / c.srai64 / c.srli64 are
# RV128-only encodings that are hints on RV64.  A hint has no
# architectural register effect, so recording none is the correct answer
# and LLVM is describing the bit pattern rather than the semantics.
#   20 rows
riscv64 FR-rd-missing c.addi +REG_FP_REG

# =====================================================================
# x86_64
# =====================================================================
#
# TRIAGED 2026-08-22 (#169).  1,339 rows from a full
# `--layer=fields --classes=MBR` sweep over 1,248,657,408 encodings, the
# observed residual EXACTLY, grouped below into seven families each of
# which names its mechanism and cites the probe that establishes it.
#
# READ THE SUMMARY LINE WITH THE ROWS.  That sweep also reports
# `size_gap=358/6430995`: encodings where Capstone and LLVM consumed a
# different number of bytes, which the tool now refuses to score because
# the two decoders described different instructions (an `xrelease xchg`
# against a lone `xrelease` prefix).  Those 6.4 M encodings are NOT
# vouched for by this file; the length disagreement itself is class D's,
# gated in isaxcheck_allow.txt, where 359 `D-size-mismatch` rows carry it.
#
# TWO OF THESE FAMILIES SHRANK BECAUSE THE BOUNDARY WAS FIXED, not
# because the rows were argued away: cap_x86_is_cmov restored CMOVcc's
# destination read and corrected FCMOVcc's exchanged operand roles, and
# the TEST / XADD implicit EFLAGS write was restored (`testb %dl, (mem)`
# published no flags write at all, and the Jcc that reads them had no
# producer in the trace).  The x86_64 baseline block in isaxcheck_allow.txt
# had been carrying `R-wr-missing testb +flags` as residual.

# (a) SEGMENT OVERRIDES THAT DO NOTHING IN 64-BIT MODE.  In long mode the
# CS / DS / ES / SS bases are architecturally zero and an override prefix
# selecting one of them changes no address; only FS and GS have a base.
# LLVM MC prints the prefix and lists the segment register as read for
# every one of them, Capstone folds the ignored ones away, and the
# dependency model follows Capstone.
#
# MEASURED, three probes, one per case:
#   --hex=648b00  `movl %fs:(%rax), %eax`  fields SRC{REG_GPR0,REG_SEG3},
#                 llvm the same -- the override that HAS a base reaches the
#                 trace on both sides, so this is not the tracer being
#                 blind to segments;
#   --hex=3e8b00  `movl %ds:(%rax), %eax`  fields SRC{REG_GPR0}, llvm adds
#                 REG_SEG0 -- the disagreement, and the prefix is inert;
#   --hex=8b00    no prefix, the two agree.
# A dependency on a register whose value cannot affect the address is not
# a dependency, so the tracer is right and the rows are LLVM's spelling.
# The same difference with the arrow reversed (`FR-rd-phantom movw +REG_SEG#`
# and the pushes) is the tracer carrying the %fs override into the operand
# the instruction reads while LLVM keeps it only in the memory operand.
#
# GRANULARITY.  These rows name the SEGMENT REGISTER, not the bank.  They
# used to read `+REG_SEG#`, because the signature key elided every bank
# index; REG_SEG0..5 are six architecturally distinct registers, so that
# elision handed back exactly the conflation the split IDs exist to
# prevent -- one row justified for an inert override licensed all six, and
# a defect that swapped one segment for another keyed identically to the
# correct mapping.  The index is now elided only for an INDEXED FILE
# (generic_reg_is_indexed_file(), champsim_tracer_generic_ids.h), where the
# members are interchangeable and one defect would otherwise mint 32 rows.
#
# What the finer key states, and the sweep now enforces line by line: every
# `FR-rd-missing` row below is REG_SEG0 (cs), REG_SEG1 (ds), REG_SEG2 (es)
# or REG_SEG5 (ss) -- the four whose base is zero in 64-bit mode -- and NOT
# ONE is REG_SEG3 (fs) or REG_SEG4 (gs).  The live-base overrides agree on
# both sides.  Under `+REG_SEG#` that discriminator could only be checked
# by hand, off to the side of the gate; it is now the allowlist itself.
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcb +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcb +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcb +REG_SEG2
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcb +REG_SEG5
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcl +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcl +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcl +REG_SEG2
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcl +REG_SEG5
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcq +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcq +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcw +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcw +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcw +REG_SEG2
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adcw +REG_SEG5
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addb +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addb +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addb +REG_SEG2
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addb +REG_SEG5
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addl +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addl +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addl +REG_SEG2
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addl +REG_SEG5
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addps +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing addps +REG_SEG1

# (b) THE x87 CONTROL WORD, which the trace now carries.  There is still
# no X86_REG_FPCW in Capstone 6.0.0-Alpha7's register enumeration (grep
# subprojects/capstone/include/capstone/x86.h: FPSW is there, FPCW is
# not), so the word does not arrive as a member of the register file; it
# arrives as a SYSTEM register from the boundary, and REG_FPCW is the
# generic id it lands on.  The FLDCW -> x87-operation edge this block used
# to record as absent is in the trace: `fldcw (%rax)` writes REG_FPCW and
# `fadds (%rax)` reads it, on separate ids from the status word every x87
# instruction WRITES, so the edge exists and no FP program serialises
# through a false one.
#
# The 111 `FN-unmapped-llvm-reg <mnem> fpcw` rows that used to sit here
# are gone, and their disappearance is not the point -- the point is that
# they were never a comparison.  A row saying "this tool cannot name the
# register" would have read exactly the same if the boundary had lost the
# operand outright.  build_name_to_generic() now indexes the token against
# the tracer's own role mapping, so the two sides are compared and the
# residue below is what they actually disagree about.
#
# WHAT IS LEFT IS LLVM'S BLANKET USE.  MCInstrDesc attaches FPCW to the
# whole x87 space including the pure bit-pattern moves, whose QEMU helper
# hands no float_status to anything: FLD / FST / FSTP %st(i)
# (helper_fmov_*), FXCH, FLD1 and FLDZ, and the m80 pair FLDT / FSTPT
# (do_fldt / do_fstt copy the encoding).  `FR-rd-phantom fbld
# +REG_FCSR,REG_FPCW` is the same disagreement reversed -- helper_fbld_ST0
# does pass &env->fp_status to int64_to_floatx80, and LLVM, which names
# FPCW on the identical `fildl`, does not name it here.
#
# `FR-wr-missing fnstenv +REG_FCSR` is what is LEFT of a row that used to
# read `+REG_FCSR,REG_FPCW`.  The control-word half was a QEMU defect, not
# a decode one: SDM Vol.1 8.1.10 has FNSTENV mask every FP exception after
# the save and do_fstenv() (target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c) assigned nothing,
# so the helper now sets the masks and the boundary records the write --
# see tests/tcg/i386/test-i386-fnstenv.c, which fails on the old helper and
# passes on the new one and on real hardware.  The REG_FCSR half is LLVM's:
# it marks FNSTENV as defining FPSW, and neither the SDM nor QEMU has the
# instruction modify the status word.
x86_64 FM-miss-store cldemote
x86_64 FM-miss-store prefetchwt1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fld +REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fld1 +REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fldt +REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fldt +REG_FPCW,REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fldt +REG_FPCW,REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fldz +REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fst +REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fst +REG_FPCW,REG_FPR#
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fstp +REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fstp +REG_FPCW,REG_FPR#
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fstpt +REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fstpt +REG_FPCW,REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fstpt +REG_FPCW,REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fxch +REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom fbld +REG_FCSR,REG_FPCW
x86_64 FR-wr-missing fldcw +REG_FCSR
x86_64 FR-wr-missing fnstenv +REG_FCSR


# (c) THE TRACER'S OWN CONTROL-FLOW TAXONOMY, which is a decision rather
# than a disagreement.  A trap (INT / INT3 / SYSCALL / SYSENTER) and an
# exception return (IRET) end a true basic block: the BB chain has to seal
# there or the block would run through an entry into the handler.  The
# dependency model therefore classifies them as flow, LLVM's per-opcode
# branch flags do not, and the mipsel trap block earlier in this file is
# the same decision on another ISA.
#
# REP-prefixed string operations are here for the second half of the same
# reason: a REP is an architectural loop, the tracer publishes it as one,
# and LLVM's description of `rep movsb` carries no branch flag.
#
# The register rows that ride along (`+REG_SP`, `+REG_FLAGS` on int / iret)
# are the implicit stack and flag traffic of a trap, which the tracer
# records and LLVM's MCInstrDesc does not name.
#
# A HOST/GUEST TRANSITION IS THE SAME DECISION, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.  The
# guest-to-host half has been here since this block was written: `vmcall`
# and `vmmcall` cause a VM exit, the BB must seal, and LLVM's per-opcode
# flags say nothing about it.  The host-to-guest half -- `vmlaunch`,
# `vmresume` and `vmrun` -- is the identical transition read the other way:
# a VM entry loads the guest RIP out of the VMCS/VMCB, so the instruction
# after the entry is not the instruction after the encoding, and a chain
# that does not seal there runs a host block on into guest text.  They were
# missing from this file only because 235bc1bf04 gave them their
# BRANCH_SYSCALL_TYPE without re-running the gate that scores it (PASS 70,
# FINDING 70-B); the classification they were given is the one this block
# already describes.  iced-x86 agrees and calls all three FlowControl=CALL
# (exec120/vmx/iced.tsv); LLVM's MCInstrDesc does not, which is the
# `llvm-no` on every row here.
x86_64 FB-flow-presence int cs-yes llvm-no
x86_64 FB-flow-presence int3 cs-yes llvm-no
x86_64 FB-flow-presence rep insb cs-yes llvm-no
x86_64 FB-flow-presence rep insl cs-yes llvm-no

# (d) THE IMPLICIT STACK AND COUNTER OPERANDS OF CALL / RET / PUSH / POP /
# LOOP / REP, which LLVM's instruction descriptions omit and the tracer
# records.  `--hex=e200` is the clean case: `loop 0x100002` decrements RCX
# and branches on it, the fields side records SRC{REG_GPR1} DST{REG_GPR1},
# and LLVM's LOOP names neither -- llvmRD{-} llvmWR{-}.  A CALL that does
# not write RSP and a RET that does not read it are the same omission.
# The tracer is right in every row here; LLVM is modelling what its
# register allocator needs to know, which is not the same question.

# (e) mayLoad / mayStore AS AN LLVM SCHEDULING PROPERTY.  Same family as
# the aarch64 barrier block and the mipsel DSP block earlier in this file:
# LLVM sets both bits on fences, hints, transactional-memory ops and cache
# maintenance to keep the scheduler from moving memory across them, and
# they touch no memory an address could name.  Capstone reports no memory
# operand and the fields side reserves no lane.
#
# The other direction in this family is the memory the tracer DOES reserve
# a lane for and LLVM does not describe: the stack traffic of CALL / RET /
# ENTER, the string operations' own operands, and the descriptor loads of
# INVLPG / INVEPT / INVPCID / INVVPID.  Those accesses are real -- QEMU's
# memory callbacks deliver them -- so the lane is right and the row is
# LLVM's silence.
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none invept
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none invlpg
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none invpcid
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none invvpid
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lfsl
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lfsq
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lfsw
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lgdtq
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lgs
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lgsl
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lgsw
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lidtq
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lssl
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lssq
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none lssw
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none sgdtq
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none sidtq
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none smsww
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none vmclear
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none vmptrld
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none vmptrst
x86_64 FM-cs-mem-llvm-none vmxon

# (f) THE x87 STACK, where LLVM names the explicit operand and nothing
# else.  `fadd %st(0), %st(1)` reads both stack slots; LLVM's description
# lists only ST(i).  FCMOVcc reads its old destination and writes ST(0);
# LLVM names neither.  FXAM and FTST read ST(0); LLVM names neither.  The
# tracer follows Capstone, which names the implicit ST(0) in every one of
# these cases, and is right.
#
# EVERY ROW HERE NOW CARRIES REG_FCSR AS WELL, because the boundary states
# the read of TOP.  QEMU addresses the x87 stack through
# `#define ST0 (env->fpregs[env->fpstt].d)` (target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c),
# so a form that names a slot depends on whatever last wrote the TOP field
# of the status word -- an `fld` in between makes the next `%st(0)` a
# different physical register, which is the R7 regfile-dependency test
# answered yes.  The tracer folds the status word onto REG_FCSR; LLVM MC
# names no TOP read on any x87 form, so 69 `+REG_FPR#` signatures became
# `+REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#` and 13 encodings acquired a `+REG_FCSR` signature.
# The claim is derived rather than asserted: x87_cw_derive.py expands those
# macros and walks fpu_helper.c to a fixed point over an OBSERVED
# `-one-insn-per-tb -d op` dump, and the boundary agrees with it on 151 of
# its 153 x87 encodings -- the two it does not are FNOP and FWAIT, which
# read env->fpus for the exception-summary bit rather than for TOP and name
# no stack slot.
#
# The FCMOVcc rows read against a boundary this arc CORRECTED: Capstone
# reported that family with its operand roles exchanged (ST(0) read,
# ST(i) written, for an instruction that moves ST(i) into ST(0)), and
# cap_x86_is_cmov now forces the destination read-write and the source
# read.  Before that fix the trace published a definition of a register
# FCMOVcc never writes.
#
# ONE ROW IS A DEFECT, NOT A DIFFERENCE: `FR-wr-phantom fxam +REG_FPR#`.
# Capstone's implicit regs_write for FXAM (`--hex=d9e5`) contains ST(0),
# and the SDM is explicit that FXAM classifies the value in ST(0) and sets
# C0/C2/C3 without modifying it.  The trace carries a definition that
# never happened.  Left uncorrected deliberately -- one instruction,
# absent from compiled code -- and named so it cannot be read as vouched
# for.  Boundary twin and the full argument: family (e) in
# isaxcheck_allow.txt.
x86_64 FM-miss-load fxsave
x86_64 FM-miss-load fxsave64
x86_64 FM-miss-store fxrstor
x86_64 FM-miss-store fxrstor64

# (g) REAL GAPS INHERITED FROM CAPSTONE'S OPERAND REPORTING, named as
# defects rather than vouched for.  Each of these is a dependency edge the
# hardware has and the trace does not; each goes DEAD when the
# execution-derived register capture of the behavioural-oracle arc lands,
# and the gate then demands its removal.  Verified one probe each:
#
#   adox (--hex=f30f38f6c3)  destination read missing: ADOX is
#     dest = dest + src + OF and Capstone marks the destination write-only.
#   bswapw (--hex=660fcd)  same shape: the operand it byte-swaps is
#     reported write-only.
#   rcl / rcr (--hex=c0142e00)  rotate THROUGH CARRY without reading the
#     carry flag -- the implicit EFLAGS read is absent.
#   clc / stc / cmc / cli / sti (--hex=f8, --hex=fa)  the flag-writing
#     forms report either no read (they preserve the other flag bits) or,
#     for CLI/STI, no access at all.
#   xlatb (--hex=d7)  Capstone reports NO operands whatever; XLAT reads
#     RBX and AL and writes AL.
#   cmpxchg8b / cmpxchg16b (--hex=0fc708)  the RCX:RBX new-value pair is
#     not reported read and RDX is not reported written; only RAX is.
#   pcmpestrm / pcmpistrm and their VEX forms (--hex=660f3a600000)  the
#     XMM0 result and the EFLAGS write are BOTH absent, and PCMPESTR's
#     EDX:EAX length pair is not reported read.  Capstone reports the
#     instruction as writing nothing at all.
#   the scalar SSE merges -- cvtsd2ss, cvtss2sd, cvtsi2ss, cvtsi2sd,
#     sqrtss, sqrtsd, rcpss, rsqrtss (--hex=f30f5100)  these preserve the
#     upper lanes of their destination, so the destination is an input;
#     Capstone reports it write-only.
#   the AVX2 gathers  the destination's merge read is reported the same
#     way (the mask WRITE is already repaired at the boundary).
#   rdsspd / rdsspq (--hex=f30f1ec8)  a NOP when CET is off, so the
#     destination survives and is read; reported write-only.
#   shld / shrd with a memory destination (--hex=0fa5041c)  the store is
#     lost, which is the `M-miss-store shld*` family in the boundary
#     allowlist seen one layer up.
#   emms / femms (--hex=0f77)  LLVM models the tag-word invalidate as a
#     write of all eight x87 and all eight MMX registers; Capstone reports
#     no operands.  Neither decoder has a name for the tag word itself.
#   clzero / monitorx / mwaitx / pconfig / rdpkru / wrpkru / tpause /
#     umwait  the implicit general-purpose operands of the `0f 01` mod=11
#     column, which Capstone returns no operands for; boundary family (f).
#   clwb / xsave / movdir64b / movdiri / movntq / wrss* / wruss* /
#     vmaskmov* / vpmaskmov* / ptwrite  memory-direction rows of the same
#     species as the ones already repaired at the boundary, not yet
#     repaired.
#
# AND FOUR CAPSTONE MISIDENTIFICATIONS, where the two decoders are not
# looking at the same instruction, so the register sets differ for a
# reason that is not about registers at all:
#   --hex=66f30f2a00   Capstone `cvtpi2pd`, LLVM `cvtsi2ssl`.  Both 66 and
#     F3 are mandatory prefixes; the last one wins, so LLVM is right and
#     Capstone dispatched on the wrong one.  Same for cvtpd2pi / cvttpd2pi.
#   --hex=f3480fc7f0   Capstone `rdrandl`, LLVM `senduipi` -- F3 0F C7 /6
#     is SENDUIPI, and Capstone consumes the F3 without dispatching on it.
#     Identical in shape to the CLUI / STUI row already recorded as family
#     (i) in isaxcheck_allow.txt.
#   --hex=66480fae3412 Capstone `clwb`, LLVM `xsaveopt64`, same
#     mandatory-prefix priority defect.
#   --hex=f3480f7e00   Capstone `movq (%rax), %xmm0`, LLVM `movq %mm0,
#     (%rax)`.  Here LLVM is the one that is wrong -- GNU binutils reads it
#     Capstone's way -- and the boundary comment carries the argument.
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adoxl +REG_FP_REG
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adoxl +REG_GPR#
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adoxl +REG_SP
x86_64 FR-rd-missing adoxq +REG_FP_REG

# =====================================================================
# mipsel -- the register facts the boundary supplies and LLVM does not
# =====================================================================
#
# These are the fields-layer face of the four boundary corrections landed
# in b35235c909 and 7d13a130f8; the boundary-layer face is the matching
# block in isaxcheck_allow.txt, which carries the full justification for
# each.  They appear here as well because the fields layer inherits the
# boundary's registers -- that inheritance is the point, and the two
# blocks moving together is what says the plugin passed the fact through
# instead of inventing or dropping it.
#
#   +REG_FCSR   FCR31 / MSACSR on COP1 and MSA arithmetic.  QEMU's
#               update_fcr31() read-modify-writes the word on every call
#               (SET_FP_CAUSE, target/mips/cpu.h:83) and reads
#               GET_FP_ENABLE from it; update_msacsr() is the same shape.
#               LLVM's MIPS tables model no FP status register at all.
#   +REG_SYS*   the CP0 registers an instruction owns, in the class the
#               split put them in -- REG_SYSMMU for the TLB file,
#               REG_SYSEXC for Status/Cause/EPC, REG_SYSDBG for the EJTAG
#               file.  QEMU's helpers name them one at a time.
#   +REG_SYSEXC the footprint of the exception an instruction RAISES
#     on traps   (R7.6): syscall, break and the twelve conditional traps
#               reach set_EPC, which read-modify-writes Status and Cause
#               and writes EPC (tlb_helper.c:1420-1456).  sdbbp raises
#               EXCP_DBp instead and carries REG_SYSDBG on top, which is
#               why its row is the one that names two ids.  LLVM MC
#               models no exception footprint on any of them, so every
#               one of these is a phantom on both sides -- the read and
#               the write, because the model is a read-modify-write.
#               This is NOT extended to instructions that can merely
#               FAULT; see the reasoning at the switch in disas/capstone.c.
#   mftr/mttr   the MT ASE far operand, reported in the file the (u, sel)
#               pair selects instead of as a GPR.  The paired phantom and
#               missing entries are ONE operand reclassified.
#   lwle/lwre   the EVA unaligned loads merge into $rt, so $rt is a read;
#               LLVM's MCInstrDesc agrees, only its MCInst drops the tie.
#
# The trap block below is the ONE place a deletion here would be wrong:
# LLVM will not start modelling MIPS exception entry, so these rows do not
# expire the way the FCR31 ones would.  They go when the tracer stops
# recording the footprint, and R7.6 says it should not.
#
# HOW TO DELETE: each of these disappears exactly when LLVM MC starts
# modelling the fact, at which point the gate's dead-rule arm fails on
# the entry.  Spot-checks: `--hex=00290646` (add.s), `--hex=02000042`
# (tlbwi), `--hex=00200541` (mftc0), `--hex=1900a47c` (lwle).
mipsel FR-rd-phantom add.d +REG_FCSR
mipsel FR-wr-phantom add.d +REG_FCSR

# THE TRAPPING ARITHMETIC, the same class as the traps below and admitted
# on the same fact: add, addi, sub and their 64-bit siblings raise on
# signed overflow and reach the same `set_EPC:` body, so the exception file
# is part of what the OPCODE does -- MIPS ships each as a PAIR whose only
# difference is the trap (add/addu, addi/addiu, sub/subu), and the `u`
# member is in neither list.  LLVM MC models no MIPS exception entry at
# all.  `neg` is Capstone's spelling of `sub rd,$zero,rt`; the rows key on
# the mnemonic, the boundary keys on the instruction id.

# =====================================================================
# x86_64 -- the system registers the boundary supplies and LLVM does not
# =====================================================================
#
# R7.2 and the x86 half of R7.5.  Twelve x86 system registers reach the
# dependency model through disas/capstone.c as QEMU_PLUGIN_OP_SYSREG
# operands, because Capstone's x86 register enum has an id for none of
# them: GDTR, IDTR, LDTR and TR (REG_SYSMMU), MXCSR and the x87 control
# and tag words (REG_FCSR), the MSR file, IA32_TSC_AUX and XCR0
# (REG_SYS), the time-stamp counter (REG_SYSTIMER) and the CET
# shadow-stack pointer (REG_SSP).  The registers come from QEMU's own
# modelling of each instruction, cited register by register at the
# switch in disas/capstone.c.
#
# THE DELETION CONDITION FOR THIS BLOCK DIFFERS FROM ITS NEIGHBOURS' and
# it is worth writing down.  LLVM MC names two of these registers and no
# more -- MXCSR on LDMXCSR / STMXCSR, and the x87 status word -- so
# every other row here is a phantom to LLVM in the same permanent sense
# the mipsel CP0 rows are: LLVM's x86 tables model no descriptor-table
# register, no MSR file, no TSC and no SSP, and there is no reason to
# expect them to start.  These rows expire if the TRACER stops recording
# the footprint, not because the reference catches up.
#
#   +REG_SYSMMU   sgdt/lgdt, sidt/lidt, sldt/lldt, str/ltr.  QEMU stores
#                 and loads gdt.base / idt.base in the translator
#                 (translate.c:3318, :3373, :3524, :3540) and
#                 helper_lldt / helper_ltr write env->ldt / env->tr
#                 (seg_helper.c:1288, :1342).  The lldt / ltr rows carry
#                 the id on BOTH sides because those two helpers also
#                 READ env->gdt -- `dt = &env->gdt` at :1304 and :1359 --
#                 the selector they take being an index into the GDT.
#   +REG_FCSR     ldmxcsr/stmxcsr and their VEX forms, fldcw/fnstcw,
#                 fninit, fnsave/frstor, fxsave/fxrstor, ffree/ffreep.
#                 env->mxcsr, env->fpuc and env->fptags[]; the three
#                 share the FPCTRL role and therefore one id, so the
#                 rows name the id once however many of the three the
#                 instruction moves.
#                 `FR-rd-phantom fnstcw +REG_FCSR` is the opposite
#                 shape and is a REFERENCE over-read: LLVM (and XED)
#                 model FNSTCW as WRITING the x87 status word.  It does
#                 not -- QEMU's FNSTCW only reads env->fpuc
#                 (fpu_helper.c:757) and the SDM gives it no status
#                 effect -- so the tracer's read is the phantom only in
#                 LLVM's frame.
#   +REG_SYS      rdmsr/wrmsr/rdpmc, rdpid and rdtscp's second result,
#                 xgetbv/xsetbv and the twelve XSAVE / XRSTOR forms.
#                 The MSR is selected at RUNTIME by ECX, so one id for
#                 the file is what a decode can honestly say; IA32_TSC_AUX
#                 is an MSR and lands there for that reason, and XCR0 is
#                 read by every xsave/xrstor because `rfbm &= env->xcr0`
#                 gates each component (fpu_helper.c:2795, :3077).
#   +REG_SYSTIMER rdtsc and rdtscp.  A counter that advances on its own
#                 is its own dependency population -- the same argument
#                 that put MIPS Count and Compare on this id.
#   +REG_SSP      EIGHT instructions, in the direction each of them has:
#                 read on rdsspd/rdsspq; read and write on incsspd/
#                 incsspq, rstorssp and saveprevssp; read and write plus
#                 the MSR the value comes from on setssbsy; write on
#                 clrssbsy.  QEMU's TCG i386 target does not model CET,
#                 so these facts are the SDM's and are recorded because
#                 the set is architectural (R2); REG_SSP is the id
#                 RISC-V Zicfiss `ssp` already carries (R8.6).  The
#                 per-instruction SDM step that produces each access is
#                 cited at the CET arm of the switch in
#                 disas/capstone.c.
#                 THESE ROWS ARE NOT "LLVM IS SILENT".  LLVM names `ssp`
#                 on exactly these instructions and agrees with the
#                 tracer about them.  The rows exist because
#                 is_dropped_reg() drops `ssp` from the REFERENCE side,
#                 for a reason that has nothing to do with CET decode:
#                 LLVM also names it on every CALL, modelling the
#                 shadow-stack push that QEMU does not perform and the
#                 tracer's CALL does not record.  The drop is therefore
#                 asymmetric at this layer -- the tracer's REG_SSP
#                 survives and LLVM's `ssp` does not -- and these rows
#                 are that asymmetry.
#                 WHAT THE ASYMMETRY COSTS, now measured rather than
#                 assumed.  It makes BOTH layers blind to a MISSING
#                 tracer-side SSP: this layer discards LLVM's `ssp`
#                 before the compare so no FR-*-missing row can form,
#                 and the boundary layer drops the token on both sides
#                 (isaxcheck.cc is_dropped_reg, isa==ISA_X86_64) so it
#                 sees nothing either way.  Four attribution defects
#                 lived there until 2026-08-23 -- saveprevssp's write,
#                 rstorssp's read, and setssbsy and clrssbsy entirely --
#                 with both arms exiting 0 and dead_allow_rules=0
#                 throughout.  The three rows added with them
#                 (FR-rd-phantom rstorssp / setssbsy, FR-wr-phantom
#                 clrssbsy / saveprevssp / setssbsy) are what the repair
#                 looks like from this side.  Making the drop symmetric
#                 would trade the noise for blindness in the other
#                 direction, which is why it is left this way; it
#                 deletes when CALL's own shadow-stack footprint is
#                 ruled on, and that ruling is what closes the hole.
#   rdsspq rows   move from FR-wr-missing to FR-rd-missing, joining
#                 rdsspd's, because X86_INS_RDSSPQ was classified
#                 GEN_OP_NOP and is now GEN_OP_MOV like its 32-bit
#                 sibling.  The justification is family (g)'s and is
#                 unchanged: with CET off the instruction is a NOP, so
#                 LLVM models the destination as surviving and therefore
#                 read.
#   +REG_SEG#     the fnsave / fxsave / fxsave64 rows lose their REG_FCSR
#                 half and keep the segment half: the segment-base read
#                 residual is family (b)'s and is untouched by this.
#   xsave         `+REG_GPR#,REG_SYS` is the decode-disagreement row
#                 (Capstone reads `xsave`, LLVM reads `ptwriteq`) with
#                 the new id folded in; it is family (a)'s row, repointed.
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fnsave +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fnsave +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fxsave +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fxsave +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fxsave64 +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing fxsave64 +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing rdsspq +REG_FP_REG
x86_64 FR-rd-missing rdsspq +REG_GPR#
x86_64 FR-rd-missing rdsspq +REG_SP
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom incsspd +REG_SSP
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom incsspq +REG_SSP

x86_64 FR-rd-phantom rdsspd +REG_SSP
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom rdsspq +REG_SSP

x86_64 FR-rd-phantom rdtscp +REG_SYS,REG_SYSTIMER
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom rstorssp +REG_SSP
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom saveprevssp +REG_SSP
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom setssbsy +REG_SSP,REG_SYS
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom sgdtq +REG_SYSMMU
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom sidtq +REG_SYSMMU
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom sldtl +REG_SYSMMU
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom sldtq +REG_SYSMMU
x86_64 FR-rd-phantom sldtw +REG_SYSMMU

# =====================================================================
# riscv64: the boundary repairs of the RVV / Zfa / Zicsr arcs, which LLVM
# MC has no description for.  ADDED 2026-08-23, closing the gap COVERAGE.md
# 6.2 items 1-2 named: the repairs landed and this file was never extended,
# so the whole riscv64 arm had been failing rather than gating.
#
# EVERY BLOCK BELOW NAMES THE CODE THAT PRODUCES THE TERM AND THE AUTHORITY
# THAT VOUCHES FOR IT.  None of them is a comparison that passed: LLVM's
# instruction descriptions carry no implicit CSR access and no vector
# context at all, so these terms cannot appear on its side for any
# subtarget.  What DOES vouch for them is tools/implicit_audit.py, whose
# riscv64 expectations come from the Sail model.
# =====================================================================

# --- The RVV and FP context terms.  Five separate repairs meet in these
#     signatures, and a qualifier can carry several at once:
#
#   +v# on the READ side   an RVV vector DESTINATION is also a SOURCE.
#       Tail-undisturbed (vtype.vta=0), mask-undisturbed (vtype.vma=0) and
#       prestart (elements below vstart) each leave part of vd standing,
#       and all three are settled at runtime by state a preceding vsetvli
#       wrote -- so there is no encoding for which the read can be ruled
#       out.  Sail folds vd_val into init_masked_result before the element
#       loop; QEMU's vector helpers run `for (i = env->vstart; i < vl; i++)`
#       and then refill the tail from vd via vext_set_elems_1s().
#       Produced by the cap_riscv_is_vector_encoding() block in
#       disas/capstone.c.
#
#   +v# on either side     the nf group expansion.  `vlseg6e64.v v8, (a1)`
#       transfers six fields into six consecutive vector register groups
#       and Capstone names only the base (RVV v1.0 sec 7.8; Sail's
#       VLSEGTYPE loops read_vreg_seg over nf).  Without the expansion the
#       consumer of v13 never sees the load that wrote it.
#
#   +vctrl on the WRITE side   every RVV instruction CLEARS vstart.
#       RVV v1.0 sec 3.7: "the vstart CSR is reset to zero at the end of
#       execution of any vector instruction".  Sail closes every vector
#       execute clause with set_vstart(zeros()); QEMU writes
#       env->vstart = 0 at the tail of every vector helper.  vl, vtype and
#       vstart share QEMU_PLUGIN_SYSREG_VECCTRL, so they meet on one
#       generic slot and this is that slot's producer -- before it, an
#       instruction that trapped mid-vector resumed off a value the trace
#       said nobody wrote.
#
#   +fcsr on BOTH sides    the accrued exception flags.  fflags is
#       ACCUMULATED (`fcsr[FFLAGS] | flags` in Sail's accrue_fflags), so
#       the access is read-modify-write: an FP instruction depends on the
#       flags standing when it starts and leaves the union behind.  The
#       vector fixed-point saturation flag vxsat is the same shape.
#       Nothing reported it before -- so an fdiv.s that raised DZ and the
#       frflags that read it back had no dependency between them at all.
#
#   +frm                   the dynamic rounding mode an FP operation
#       encoded with rm = 111 reads.  Sail records it; LLVM models no frm
#       use.  It appears beside +fcsr rather than instead of it: the two
#       are different fields of the same word and the boundary now reports
#       both, which is why the bare `+frm` rows this block replaces went
#       dead -- their term is still there, inside a wider qualifier.
#
#   +vl,vtype              the vector configuration read.
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom feq.d +REG_FCSR
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom feq.h +REG_FCSR
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom feq.s +REG_FCSR

# --- Zimop reads NOTHING, and LLVM says it reads its operands.
#     An unimplemented May-Be-Operation is defined to write zero to rd and
#     read neither source.  QEMU says it in one line --
#     `gen_set_gpr(ctx, a->rd, ctx->zero)`, trans_mop_r_n / trans_mop_rr_n
#     in target/riscv/insn_trans/trans_rvzimop.c.inc -- and Sail marks the
#     parameters `_rs1` / `_rs2` unused.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc lists rs1/rs2
#     as operands and therefore as uses, which is a description of the
#     ENCODING, not of the effect.  Keeping LLVM's reads would fabricate a
#     dependency on registers the instruction never looks at.
#     Removable when LLVM stops modelling the unused operands as uses.
riscv64 FR-rd-missing mop.r.0 +REG_GPR#
riscv64 FR-rd-missing mop.r.0 +REG_LR
riscv64 FR-rd-missing mop.r.0 +REG_SP

riscv64 FR-rd-missing mop.r.1 +REG_LR
riscv64 FR-rd-missing mop.r.1 +REG_SP
