# --- Zicfilp `lpad` reads x7 and consumes the landing-pad state.
#     A landing pad checks the label the indirect branch left in x7 (t2)
#     against the one in its own encoding, traps on a mismatch, and clears
#     the expected-landing-pad state the branch set.  ELP has no CSR
#     address of its own -- its architectural homes are the status word's
#     MPELP / SPELP fields -- so the state effect is named on mstatus and
#     lands on REG_SYS.  LLVM decodes `lpad` (experimental-zicfilp) but its
#     description carries neither the x7 use nor the ELP state, so the
#     whole of Zicfilp's forward-edge handshake is absent on its side.
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom lpad +REG_GPR#,REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom lpad +REG_SYS

# --- Zacas `amocas.q` names two register PAIRS and LLVM names one register
#     of each.  The 16-byte compare-and-swap on RV64 takes an even-odd pair
#     for rd (low half in rd, high half in rd+1) and another for rs2; Sail
#     models the pairs directly (rX_pair_bits / wX_pair_bits).  Capstone
#     and LLVM both report only the even register, so without the repair
#     half the compared value and half the stored value are absent and the
#     high half of the memory word is produced by nothing.
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom amocas.q +REG_GPR#
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom amocas.q +REG_GPR#,REG_LR
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom amocas.q +REG_LR
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom amocas.q +REG_LR

# --- The read-only implementation constants (REG_SYSID) reached through
#     Zicsr: vlenb, mvendorid, marchid, mimpid, mhartid.
#     This is the riscv64 twin of the aarch64 rows bf1ee25247 added, and it
#     is the SAME sentence as the general `+sys` block above, following the
#     selector into its second class: LLVM carries the CSR selector as the
#     12-bit immediate the encoding spells, so no CSR the tracer records
#     for a Zicsr access can ever appear in LLVM's register sets, whichever
#     generic class it lands in.  These lines do not record a comparison
#     that passed; they record one that structurally cannot happen.
#
#     OBSERVATION, NOT COVERED BY THESE LINES AND NOT CLOSED BY THEM: the
#     boundary reports a WRITE to a read-only CSR for the store forms --
#     `--hex=731020c2` (`csrw vlenb, zero`) gives WR{REG_SYSID}.  On
#     hardware that encoding raises an illegal-instruction exception and
#     writes nothing, so a class whose defining property is having no
#     producer acquires one.  It is PRE-EXISTING and not introduced by the
#     IDENT split -- the identical shape is already allowlisted one block
#     up for REG_SYS (`--hex=731000c0` gives WR{REG_SYS}) -- and it is
#     invisible to this gate either way, because LLVM names no register on
#     that side to disagree with.  Recorded here so it is not mistaken for
#     something these lines vouch for.
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrc +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrci +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrr +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrrc +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrrci +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrrs +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrrsi +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrrw +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrrwi +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrs +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom csrsi +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrc +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrci +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrrc +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrrci +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrrs +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrrsi +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrrw +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrrwi +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrs +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrsi +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrw +REG_SYSID
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom csrwi +REG_SYSID

# --- R7.4: a CSR that decides whether an instruction TRAPS is a source.
#     A pending write to the gate must resolve before the instruction can
#     be said to do anything, which is an edge a renaming regfile has to
#     respect.  The gates named here are menvcfg (senvcfg at U, henvcfg
#     under H) for the CBO, WFI and Zicfiss enable bits and the FIOM bit
#     `fence` reads, and mstatus/mepc (sstatus/sepc) for the trap returns,
#     which also RESTORE privilege state and so write as well as read.
#     Sail puts every one of them in the encdec guard or at the head of the
#     clause.  LLVM's instruction descriptions carry no implicit CSR
#     access at all, so none of these can appear on its side.
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom fence +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom mret +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom sret +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom ssamoswap.d +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom ssamoswap.d.aq +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom ssamoswap.d.aqrl +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom ssamoswap.d.rl +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom ssamoswap.w +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom ssamoswap.w.aq +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom ssamoswap.w.aqrl +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom ssamoswap.w.rl +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom sspopchk +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-rd-phantom sspush +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom mret +REG_SYS
riscv64 FR-wr-phantom sret +REG_SYS

# --- R7.4 on AArch64: the FP / SIMD / SVE / SME enable gate is a source.
#     CPACR_EL1.FPEN, CPTR_EL2.FPEN and CPTR_EL3.TFP decide whether these
#     instructions execute or trap, so a pending write to one has to
#     resolve before any of them may proceed -- the edge R7 says a
#     renaming regfile must respect.  The boundary records all three (R4:
#     which gate applies is a runtime value, so a static set names every
#     candidate); the three share REG_SYSFPEN, so one token appears.
#     LLVM MC carries no legality axis -- an instruction description has
#     no notion of being enabled -- so this read is a phantom to it on
#     every row here and can never be anything else.  The 140 FP-datapath
#     rows that also read the rounding-mode word were +REG_FCSR before
#     this and are RE-POINTED to +REG_FCSR,REG_SYSFPEN above rather than
#     added here; the one that also reads the ZA array is `zero`.

# The AArch64 system-instruction group -- the fields-layer twin of the

# --- The AArch64 system-register file, split by dependence behaviour.
#     cap_aarch64_sysreg_class used to answer OTHER for everything but
#     NZCV, FPCR/FPSR/FPMR, TPIDR_EL0/RO and 25 ID constants, so one
#     generic ID carried 1,182 architecturally distinct registers.  It now
#     answers the behaviour group -- exception state, translation, timer,
#     performance, debug, the cache and error record windows, the guarded
#     control stack pointer, vector configuration -- so MRS/MSR produce
#     one signature per group where they produced one in total.  LLVM MC
#     models no system register on these forms, so every group is a
#     phantom to it exactly as REG_SYS already was; the pre-existing
#     +REG_SYS rows stay live because 160 encodings are still residual.
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSCACHE
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSDBG
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSEXC
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSMMU
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSPERF
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSTIMER
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SSP
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSCACHE
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSDBG
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSEXC
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSMMU
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSPERF
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSTIMER
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SSP
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SYSCACHE
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SYSDBG
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SYSEXC
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SYSMMU
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SYSPERF
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_SYSTIMER
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_SYSCACHE
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_SYSDBG
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_SYSEXC
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_SYSMMU
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_SYSPERF
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_SYSTIMER

# --- FPCR joins the group, as its own register.  The FP CONTROL word left
#     REG_FCSR (the accrued-STATUS word FPSR) when the boundary began routing
#     it to QEMU_PLUGIN_SYSREG_FPCW, so MRS/MSR of the FPCR tuple produce a
#     signature of their own.  The justification is this block's: LLVM models
#     the system register as an IMMEDIATE and has no operand to name.
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_FPCW
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_FPCW
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msr +REG_FPCW
aarch64 FR-wr-phantom msrr +REG_FPCW

# --- ZT0 has a SECOND gate: SMCR_ELx.EZT0.  CheckSMEZT0Enabled reads it
#     after CheckSMEEnabled has passed, so the SME2 lookup-table forms
#     take a source on it as well.  It is NOT the FP-enable gate --
#     SMCR also carries the streaming vector length, which is what
#     REG_VCTRL names -- so the two ids stay apart.  `movt` is the one
#     mnemonic all of whose forms touch ZT0, so its +REG_SYSFPEN row is
#     re-pointed rather than kept.
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom ldr +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom luti2 +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom luti4 +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom str +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL
aarch64 FR-rd-phantom zero +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL

# --- NOT RETIRED, and the measurement says why (exec92).  Passes 44-47 each
#     recorded `zero +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL` as one of the two rules that go
#     DEAD under exec72's banked Capstone deletion, and read that as a rule
#     describing a Capstone-era operand-walk phantom that the deletion simply
#     removes.  Scored against the subject the wire actually publishes --
#     isaxcheck --srcenc over the per-encoding source corpus -- the phantom is
#     still there:
#
#       SR-rd-phantom zero +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL  n=1
#         010048c0  zero { zt0 }  wireRD{REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VCTRL} llvmRD{-}
#
#     The wire publishes exactly this pair on exactly this encoding, so the
#     row has an occupant and the ZT0 justification above is the reason it is
#     allowed.  It goes dead under the deletion because the read comparison's
#     SUBJECT vanishes there, not because the disagreement stopped happening
#     -- which is the distinction tools/isax_srcenc_gate.sh exists to draw.
x86_64 FR-rd-missing cmpxchg16b +REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing cmpxchg16b +REG_SEG1
x86_64 FR-rd-missing cmpxchg8b +REG_SEG0

x86_64 FR-rd-phantom swapgs +REG_SEG4

# --- QEMU lowers the MIPS store-conditional onto tcg_gen_atomic_cmpxchg_tl
#     (target/mips/tcg/translate.c:2219-2244), which performs a REAL load
#     before the store, and the memop callback delivers it.  Capstone and
#     LLVM both report the ARCHITECTURE, where a store-conditional writes
#     and returns a status bit and does not read -- so the load arrived
#     with no declared slot and rendered as an orphan, `ld(0x410180)`,
#     while riscv64's `sc.d` (whose Capstone MEM operand happens to carry
#     READ|WRITE) rendered the same access matched.  The decode boundary
#     now declares the load on both ISAs; this is #177's decision applied
#     where it was owed -- the trace records what executed.  `ll` / `lr.d`
#     need nothing: neither reports the phantom store #177 removed.
#     `scd` is handled in the code and is NOT listed here: it is MIPS64 and
#     this sweep is 32-bit mipsel, so a row for it would be a rule that can
#     never match -- which is what the dead-rule detector is for.
mipsel FM-extra-load sc
mipsel FM-extra-load sce


# MIPS BRANCHES DO NOT WRITE $at, AND LLVM'S MIPS TABLES SAY THEY DO.
# Every branch form -- the conditional set (beq/bne/bgez/bltz/blez/bgtz and
# their likely and zero-compare variants), the `b` and `j` macros, and the
# MSA bz.*/bnz.* forms -- carries an implicit definition of AT in LLVM's
# instruction descriptions, and Capstone reports it verbatim in regs_write.
# Both decoders agree because both read the same table.
#
# The implicit def exists for the LONG-BRANCH EXPANSION, where the ASSEMBLER
# may clobber $at while rewriting an out-of-range branch into a jump.  That
# rewrite emits its own instructions; the branch itself never touches the
# register.  Published in a trace, the write is a destination that did not
# happen: it manufactures a WAW edge against every real producer of $at and a
# RAW edge into every consumer.
#
# The dependency model drops it at the boundary (champsim_tracer_decode.cc,
# in the implicit-regs fold, gated on TRACE_ISA_MIPS and a non-NONE
# branch_type), so the fields layer records no destination where LLVM claims
# one.  These rows are that repair, enumerated exactly.
#
# Measured, not argued: gem5 -- an execution reference that decodes MIPS
# itself -- names NO destination for any of these instructions, and the
# aarch64/mipsel execution leg (tools/arc3_cov/gem5) went from 10 rows of
# `TRC-INVENTED-BRANCH-DEST` to none when the drop landed, with reg-dst-set
# agreement moving 134/147 -> 144/147 over the same run.
#
# Upstream: LLVM's MIPS branch instruction definitions should not carry
# `Defs = [AT]`; the clobber belongs to the assembler's expansion, not to the
# instruction.  Revisit on a Capstone or LLVM bump -- these rows go DEAD the
# moment either side stops claiming the write, and the gate fails on a dead
# row, which is what keeps this comment honest.
mipsel FR-wr-missing b +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing beq +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing beql +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing beqz +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing beqzl +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bgez +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bgezl +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bgtz +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bgtzl +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing blez +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing blezl +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bltz +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bltzl +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bne +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bnel +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bnez +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bnezl +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bnz.b +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bnz.d +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bnz.h +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bnz.v +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bnz.w +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bz.b +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bz.d +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bz.h +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bz.v +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing bz.w +REG_GPR#
mipsel FR-wr-missing j +REG_GPR#

# =====================================================================
# FIELDS LAYER, x86_64: the dependencies the boundary carries and LLVM
# MC's instruction descriptions do not.  Two independent groups, both
# MEASURED at the tip and neither inherited.
#
# (A) THE x87 IMPLICIT SET.  disas/capstone.c recomputes it from the
#     instruction bytes with QEMU's own gen_x87() dispatch key -- see
#     cap_x86_x87_effects -- so every ST(0)/ST(1) access and every
#     status-word read below is the operand set of the helper sequence
#     QEMU emits for that (mod, op, rm).  LLVM MC models almost none of
#     it: `fchs` carries no implicit operand although it negates ST(0),
#     `fadds (%rax)` names neither the ST(0) read nor the ST(0) write,
#     and `fst %st(1)` names st1 as a USE where the instruction assigns
#     it.  `fcmov* +REG_FLAGS` is the same shape from the other side --
#     FCMOVcc TESTS CF/ZF/PF and LLVM names no flag use.
#     CHECK: read the gen_x87() case the encoding's (mod, op, rm)
#     selects.  If the helpers there do not touch the register named in
#     the qualifier, the boundary is wrong and the entry is a bug.
#
# (B) A PRE-EXISTING RED, now closed.  Ninety-five of the signatures
#     below were unallowed at HEAD BEFORE any x87 work, with 69 dead
#     rules beside them, and the gate had not been re-run since the
#     repair that caused it: teaching the fields layer the EFLAGS
#     bitmask mapping (disas/capstone.c, cap_x86_eflags_from_bitmask)
#     turned `FR-rd-missing <shift> +REG_FLAGS` into
#     `FR-rd-phantom <shift> +REG_FLAGS` and left the old shape behind.
#     Each family is architecturally real:
#       shift/rotate +REG_FLAGS read  -- a count of 0 leaves the flags
#            UNCHANGED, so the flags word is an input (PRIOR_x -> READ)
#       rcl/rcr                       -- read CF as the rotated-in bit
#       popf +REG_FLAGS read          -- reserved bits, and IOPL/IF at
#            CPL>0, are PRESERVED, so the old word is an input
#       in/out +REG_FLAGS read        -- the #GP depends on IOPL
#       lar/lsl/verr/verw +REG_FLAGS write -- all four set ZF (SDM)
#       rsm +REG_FLAGS write          -- restores EFLAGS from SMRAM
#       encls/enclu/enclv +REG_GPR#   -- the leaf number in EAX and the
#            operands/returns in RBX/RCX/RDX; LLVM names nothing at all
#       vmfunc/xabort/xbegin +REG_GPR# -- EAX (and ECX for VMFUNC) is a
#            real operand of each; LLVM names nothing
#       pushq/pushw %fs|%gs           -- the segment READ, the stack
#            pointer, and the stack STORE, none of which LLVM models
#       +REG_SEG0/1/2/5 on the READ side -- LLVM counts a CS/DS/ES/SS
#            override as a register read; in 64-bit mode those bases are
#            architecturally ZERO, so the override cannot reach the
#            effective address and the boundary folds it away
#     CHECK: each entry names one architectural fact; delete it when
#     LLVM's MCInstrDesc starts carrying that fact.
#
# NOT COVERED HERE, deliberately: the x87 CONTROL-word (rounding-mode)
# read that LLVM names on the arithmetic forms and the boundary does
# not.  That is a separate open item, not folded into this block.

# --- NOT RETIRED, and the measurement says why (exec92).  `xbegin +REG_GPR#`
#     is the second of the two rules passes 44-47 recorded going DEAD under
#     the banked Capstone deletion.  Under the wire's own source subject it
#     does not fire -- but it is not scored either: the sled corpus carries
#     ZERO `xbegin` encodings on both arms (no `c7f8*` key in either), so the
#     mnemonic is UNREACHED, not clean.  Deleting a rule on a zero the
#     instrument was never shown able to break is the silent false success
#     this tree keeps relearning, so the row stays until a corpus that
#     reaches `xbegin` answers for it.

# R7.1-SCALAR: the destination of a UNARY legacy scalar SSE form is a PURE
# write.  ROUNDSS / ROUNDSD compute the low element from the source operand
# alone; the only part of the destination that survives is the upper lane,
# and a narrow write does not make a register a source -- rename does not
# know the data-width scope of the next reader.  LLVM MC models the
# preserve as a tied operand and so names the destination a USE; XED
# declares the same thing (`REG0=XMM_R():rw`) and the project's own
# reference arm already suppresses it under R7.1-SCALAR.  Capstone reports
# these two read-modify-write ALONE in their family -- `sqrtsd`,
# `cvtsd2ss`, `cvtsi2ss` and the rest of the unary set all come back
# WRITE-only on the same operand shape -- so leaving it would put a false
# RAW edge on every `roundsd` in a trace.  Removable when LLVM stops tying
# the operand, which is not expected.
x86_64 FR-rd-missing roundsd +REG_VEC#
x86_64 FR-rd-missing roundss +REG_VEC#

# THE ENCODED-ZERO-COUNT SHIFTS AND ROTATES, at the field layer.
#
# Same class as section (4) of isaxcheck_allow.txt, seen through the
# InsnFields the dependency model records rather than through the boundary's
# register lists: `shlq $0`, `rclb $0`, `shldq $0` and the rest of the
# C0/C1 and 0F A4 / 0F AC space with a masked-zero immediate touch no flag,
# and LLVM MC's MCInstrDesc names EFLAGS for the family regardless because a
# register allocator must assume the clobber.
#
# Intel SDM states it once per page -- SAL/SAR/SHL/SHR (Vol. 2B 4-687),
# RCL/RCR/ROL/ROR (4-543), SHLD (4-706), SHRD (4-709), all in the words "if
# the count is 0, the flags are not affected" -- and the MACHINE was asked
# rather than only the page: exec102/hw/zcount.c seeds EFLAGS with 0x000 and
# 0x8D5, executes one such instruction and reads the word back, and all nine
# zero-count forms leave every arithmetic flag as seeded on both seeds while
# three count-1 firing controls move.  QEMU agrees at
# gen_shift_count(): `*count = NULL` when `(decode->immediate & mask) == 0`,
# after which the emitters return having emitted no op.
#
# The REG_SEG0 / REG_SEG1 spellings are the segment-slot difference LLVM's
# X86 memory operand always carries, now visible on these encodings because
# the flags have left the tracer's side of the comparison.
#
# Removable when LLVM MC can express a flag effect conditioned on an operand
# VALUE, which MCInstrDesc has no form for.
x86_64 FR-rd-missing rclb +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 FR-rd-missing rclb +REG_FLAGS,REG_SEG0
x86_64 FR-rd-missing rclb +REG_FLAGS,REG_SEG1

# =====================================================================
# CAUSE 3, THE `--srcenc` FORM.  ZERO-BASE SEGMENT OVERRIDE, scored against
# THE WIRE`S OWN SOURCE LIST rather than against the boundary`s decode.
#
# The class is the one already justified above for the `R-rd-missing` keys:
# LLVM`s X86 memory operand always carries a segment-register slot, so an
# explicit override makes LLVM report a read; in 64-bit mode the CS, DS, ES
# and SS bases are forced to zero and contribute nothing to the effective
# address, so QEMU computes no dependency and the wire is right not to carry
# one.  Intel SDM Vol. 1 sec. 3.7.4.1 and Vol. 2A sec. 2.2.1: in 64-bit mode
# the CS, DS, ES and SS segment-override prefixes are ignored; only FS and GS
# have effect.
#
# WHAT IS NEW IS THE SIGNATURE, NOT THE ARGUMENT.  The `--srcenc` arms score
# the wire`s published source list, which is QEMU`s ordered read list plus
# the survivor rows, and their read findings key on `SR-` rather than `R-`
# or `FR-`.  Nothing in the allowlist carried an `SR-` key at all, so this
# whole family was unallowed on eight arms that had already adjudicated it
# on the other side of the same tool.
#
# THE DISCRIMINATOR IS CHECKED AND HOLDS, and it is the reason this block is
# safe.  Every row below is `+REG_SEG0` (CS), `+REG_SEG1` (DS), `+REG_SEG2`
# (ES) or `+REG_SEG5` (SS).  NOT ONE is `+REG_SEG3` (FS) or `+REG_SEG4`
# (GS): measured over the whole eight-arm residue, every SEG3/SEG4 finding
# is in the PHANTOM direction instead -- `pushq %fs`, `rdfsbase`, `swapgs`,
# `maskmovq` -- where the wire reads the live-base register and LLVM`s
# MCInstrDesc has no operand for it.  So the family that would have been a
# real defect stays distinguishable from the benign one, which is the
# property the `+seg` -> per-register split was made to protect.
#
# ROWS WHOSE ADDED SET IS NOT PURELY A ZERO-BASE SEGMENT ARE NOT HERE.  A
# row reading `+REG_SEG0,REG_GPR#` names a general-purpose register too and
# is a different question; only rows whose entire difference is one of the
# four ignored segment registers are listed.
#
# Measured at exec103: 327 signatures, 69,375 encodings of the corpus.
# Removable when LLVM stops putting a segment slot on every X86 memory
# operand, which is not expected.
# =====================================================================
x86_64 SR-rd-missing bnd callq +REG_SEG0
x86_64 SR-rd-missing bnd callq +REG_SEG2

# =====================================================================
# THE #UD ROWS -- THE OPCODE THE TRACER REFUSES BY RULE (PASS 66)
#
# UD0, UD1 and UD2 published `GEN_OP_SYSCALL` until 7773e9a469.  They raise
# #UD, `GEN_OP_SYSCALL` is the name of a DIFFERENT trap, and the shared
# vocabulary has no word for an invalid-opcode fault -- so the ruled row now
# REFUSES the opcode and publishes `GEN_OP_UNKNOWN`, keeping the separately
# true `BRANCH_SYSCALL_TYPE` seal.
#
# The fields layer sees that refusal as `FU-unclassified`, which is the
# CORRECT report and the intended one: the tracer states no class for these
# encodings, and the gate saying so is the refusal arriving where it should.
# These two rows allow that signature and name the ruling as the reason;
# they are not excusing a gap the tracer failed to fill.
#
# FOUR ROWS WERE RETIRED FOR THIS, and the sequence is worth recording.
# They excused the signatures the OLD class produced --
# `FB-flow-presence ud{1,2} cs-yes llvm-no` and
# `FM-llvm-mem-cs-none ud{1,2} load` -- and they went dead the moment the
# class changed, because the disagreement they described stopped being
# published.  Nothing was looking for that: the eight STANDING isax arms are
# bare, a bare arm reports `dead_allow_rules=0` over rules it never scores,
# and this pass had just finished making that visible (474a32ec7c, FINDING
# 65-D).  The `--srcenc` arm named all four the first time it ran after the
# change.
#
# AND UD0 IS NOT HERE, WHICH IS THE SAME LESSON AGAIN.  A row for it was
# written with these two, on the symmetry that the ruling covers all three
# encodings -- and the gate reported it DEAD on the next run, because UD0
# (0F FF) is not in the corpus and the row licensed nothing.  That is
# exactly the mistake FINDING 65-D retired two mipsel rows for: a
# justification is necessary and a row also has to be REACHED.  The ruling
# still covers UD0; an allowlist row is not where that is recorded.
#
# TWO MORE ROWS WERE RETIRED A PASS LATER, and they are the reason the
# sentence above says FOUR and the closure said zero.  `FR-rd-missing ud1
# +REG_SEG0` and `+REG_SEG1` excused the read disagreement the OLD class
# produced.  They died with the other four and for the same reason -- and
# they outlived the closure that counted them, because that closure was
# measured on a `--srcenc` arm, which does not score the `FR-rd-*` family at
# all.  Four rows were visible to it; these two were not; `dead_allow_rules
# 4 -> 1 -> 0` was true of the arm and false of the allowlist, and the
# eight STANDING bare arms went red on them for a pass.  FINDING 66V-D.
#
# WHY NO `FR-*` SIGNATURE FOR THESE ENCODINGS CAN COME BACK while the
# refusal stands: the fields comparison reaches class R only through
# `decode_detail_to_generic()`, and on a refused opcode that returns not-ok,
# so isaxcheck emits `FU-unclassified` and RETURNS -- classes M, B and R are
# never scored for that encoding.  The two rows below are what the arm sees
# for UD1 now; there is nothing else left for a row to license.
# =====================================================================
x86_64 FU-unclassified ud1
x86_64 FU-unclassified ud2


# >>> BEGIN GENERATED SR-rd BLOCK -- isax_srcenc_rows.py --header --layer fields
# =====================================================================
# THE FIELDS LAYER'S `SR-rd-*` FAMILY -- ALL FOUR ISAs
#
# GENERATED.  `isax_srcenc_rows.py --header --layer fields` emits this
# block and the rows below it from the arm output named at the foot;
# every number is read from an arm or md5summed from the corpus, and
# nothing in it is typed.  Do not hand-correct a line here -- re-run
# the arms and re-emit, or the block goes back to being a summary that
# has to be patched to stay true.
#
# WHY A SECOND BLOCK AND NOT A COPY OF THE OTHER LAYER'S.  isaxcheck
# states in its own `--srcenc` comment that this family is
# LAYER-INDEPENDENT: "the wire's source list is one list; it is not a
# property of which decoder view this tool happens to be holding".
# Both arms therefore score the SAME CLAIMS.  What differs is REACH --
# the two decode with different views and reach different encodings --
# so each allowlist gets rows for the signatures ITS OWN arm reported.
# A row copied across would land with no occupant here, which is the
# first property this generator exists to make impossible.
#
# THE POPULATION THESE ROWS CLOSE, from the arms' own summary lines.
# Measured at this tip with BOTH generated blocks absent -- that is
# what the regeneration procedure at the foot does, and it is the only
# reading on which the number means "what these rows are for".  Both,
# and not this one alone: the two layers score the same claims and
# leaving the other in place would report this layer's residue against
# an allowlist half of it had already been closed by.  Re-running the
# arms WITH the blocks in place is the confirmation and belongs in the
# pass evidence, not here: it reads 0 by construction.
#
#   layer       x86_64  aarch64  riscv64   mipsel    total
#   fields        661     1115     1059      864     3699
#   boundary      638     1115     1017      233     3003
#
# THE LOSS-DIRECTION SIGNATURES ARE ADJUDICATED, NOT ALLOWLISTED AS-IS.
# `SR-rd-missing` says LLVM names a source the wire does not, which is
# the direction R12.1 treats as disqualifying, so none of them may be
# excused by a row that merely records the difference.  Each lands in a
# class whose comment quotes the reference fact that makes the
# REFERENCE the wrong side.  This arm's, by class:
#
#   x86_64     33  X-X87OPERAND   12
#                  X-CONSTCOUNT   8
#                  X-IMPLICITGPR  7
#                  X-SSP          6
#   aarch64     8  A-SYSSEL       2
#                  A-ERET         2
#                  A-SATQC        2
#                  A-MISC         2
#   riscv64   182  R-ZIMOP        174
#                  R-HINT         8
#   mipsel      3  M-CP0          2
#                  M-MT           1
#
# CLASSES THIS ARM GAVE NO ROWS.  A class with no occupant is not a
# clean class -- it is a class whose subject this arm did not
# reach, or one whose last member was closed -- and either way the
# name is worth being able to find:
#
#   x86_64   X-SELFRELOAD
#   aarch64  A-FROZEN
#   mipsel   M-DSPIMM
#   mipsel   M-MSACTL
#
# CORPUS: /mnt/md0/QEMU/cst_runs/exec132/srcencorp/tip132
#   x86_64    6416527 rows md5 53f4eef64f63d7537462e406cb87851b
#   aarch64   1408171 rows md5 d407a13138a9281cb541393508f20c98
#   riscv64     86889 rows md5 baf8571353b1e382588425d728c090ad
#   mipsel     548886 rows md5 7c5d6f1bdbdf7a3b43d0ad2aa4af321f
#
# THE ARMS THAT PRODUCED IT, from the gate's own roll-up:
#   SO_SHA=419e0c74b3755d13
#   ISAX_SHA=1d0ad8137585bc36
#   ALL_ARMS_DONE worst_rc=1 unscored_arms=8
#
# HOW TO REGENERATE:
#   isax_srcenc_gate.sh run <build> <arms> <corpus>
#   isax_srcenc_rows.py --header --arms <arms> --layer fields \
#       --corpus <corpus> --rc <arms>/rc.txt --replace <allowlist>
# =====================================================================
# --- X-FIP: every x87 instruction reads the CS SELECTOR, to save it as the FPU code segment.
#     205 signature(s), 395053 encodings at this tip.
#
# The x87 state includes the FPU Instruction Pointer and the FPU Code Segment
# selector -- the pair FSTENV / FSAVE / FXSAVE publish (Intel SDM Vol. 1
# sec. 8.1.8) -- and the instruction that updates FIP must read CS to do it.
# QEMU does exactly that, in one place, for the whole x87 population:
#
#     target/i386/tcg/translate.c, `if (update_fip)`:
#         tcg_gen_ld32u_tl(..., offsetof(CPUX86State, segs[R_CS].selector));
#         tcg_gen_st_tl (..., offsetof(CPUX86State, fpcs));
#
# so REG_SEG0 reaches the wire's read list on every x87 form that updates FIP,
# including the register-only ones with no memory operand at all.  LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc names no segment register on any of them.  This is NOT the
# zero-base segment-override family below: that one is about an override
# prefix the reference reports and 64-bit mode ignores, this one is a real read
# the reference does not report.  Where the qualifier names REG_FCSR beside it
# the register is FPSW, read-modify-written for the condition codes and the
# stack top, and where it names REG_FPR# it is the stack slot ST(0) resolves
# to.

# --- X-IOPL: IN and OUT read EFLAGS.IOPL to decide whether they are allowed to run.
#     6 signature(s), 289974 encodings at this tip.
#
# The I/O instructions are permitted by CPL and EFLAGS.IOPL, and outside ring 0
# by the I/O permission bitmap (Intel SDM Vol. 2A, IN / OUT: "protected mode
# ... IOPL").  QEMU states that check at translation (gen_check_io), so the
# flag register reaches the read list.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc models the data
# movement and no permission check, so it names no flag use.
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom inb +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom inl +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom inw +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom outb +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom outl +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom outw +REG_FLAGS

# --- X-REP: the REP prefix reads the count register, which is not part of the opcode LLVM describes.
#     57 signature(s), 1348191 encodings at this tip.
#
# REP / REPE / REPNE make the string operation iterate on RCX, so RCX is a true
# source of the prefixed form.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc describes the BASE OPCODE --
# MOVSB, STOSB, INSB -- and the prefix is a separate MCInst feature, so its
# read set is short by the count register on every prefixed encoding.  The
# tracer's subject is the instruction QEMU translated, prefix included.
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom loop +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom loope +REG_FLAGS,REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom loopne +REG_FLAGS,REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep insb +REG_GPR#

x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep insw +REG_GPR#

x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep lodsl +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep lodsq +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep lodsw +REG_GPR#

x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep movsl +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep movsq +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep movsw +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep outsb +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep outsl +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep outsw +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep stosb +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom rep stosl +REG_GPR#

# --- X-PARTIAL: an instruction that writes PART of a register must carry the rest forward, and the reference has one operand.
#     108 signature(s), 445305 encodings at this tip.
#
# BT / BTS / BTR / BTC write CF and leave OF, SF, ZF, AF and PF UNCHANGED
# (Intel SDM Vol. 2A, BT); SAHF writes five flags and leaves OF and the
# reserved bits; LAHF writes AH and leaves the other 56 bits of RAX; POPF
# restores some flags and preserves others by privilege.  At the granularity a
# register file schedules on, each of those is a PARTIAL WRITE, which is a
# read-modify-write: the instruction must carry the untouched part forward, and
# QEMU's lazy-flag machinery states the read.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc carries ONE
# operand for EFLAGS and one for the GPR and marks it Def, so it has no way to
# express a partial write and reports no use.

x86_64 SR-rd-phantom bsfw +REG_SP

# --- X-FSGS: the FS and GS BASES, which are the only segment bases that are live in 64-bit mode.
#     5 signature(s), 6944 encodings at this tip.
#
# The phantom direction of the segment family: the tracer reads the live base
# register -- `swapgs`, `rdfsbase`, `wrgsbase`, `maskmovq` -- and LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc has no operand for the hidden descriptor base.  This is the
# counterpart to the zero-base override family below, and the discriminator
# between them is checked: NOT ONE row here is REG_SEG0 / REG_SEG1 / REG_SEG2 /
# REG_SEG5, and not one row there is REG_SEG3 or REG_SEG4.
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom maskmovq +REG_FCSR,REG_SEG3
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom maskmovq +REG_FCSR,REG_SEG4
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom swapgs +REG_SEG4
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom xlatb +REG_SEG3
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom xlatb +REG_SEG4

# --- X-UPPERLANE: a legacy scalar or unary SSE form preserves the upper lanes of its destination, so the destination is read.
#     13 signature(s), 64601 encodings at this tip.
#
# R7.1-SCALAR, restated at this layer: the destination of a legacy scalar SSE
# form survives above the element the instruction writes, so it is an input.
# LLVM models the preserve as a tied operand on the forms whose assembly
# repeats the register and not on the rest, so its read set is short by the
# destination exactly where the tie is absent.

x86_64 SR-rd-phantom vaesdec +REG_VEC#

# --- X-CONSTCOUNT: a rotate-through-carry by an immediate ZERO reads nothing, and LLVM's description is per-opcode.
#     8 signature(s), 10730 encodings at this tip.
#
# RCL and RCR with a masked count of zero do not rotate and do not touch the
# flags (Intel SDM Vol. 2B, RCL/RCR), so QEMU's translator folds the whole
# operation away at translation time and reads no flag.  Measured on the same
# tool: `rclb $3, (%rdx)` (--hex=c01203) DOES read REG_FLAGS on both sides, so
# the disagreement is confined to the immediate-zero encodings.  LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc is keyed on the OPCODE and cannot say "reads CF unless the
# immediate is zero", so it names the use unconditionally.
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rclb +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rcll +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rclq +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rclw +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rcrb +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rcrl +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rcrq +REG_FLAGS
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rcrw +REG_FLAGS

# --- X-X87OPERAND: the x87 operand LLVM attaches and the hardware does not consult.
#     12 signature(s), 14974 encodings at this tip.
#
# FFREE / FFREEP mark a stack slot EMPTY in the tag word; they do not read the
# value in it.  FLD / FST / FSTP / FXCH between registers, FLD1 / FLDZ and the
# m80 FLDT / FSTPT pair move a bit pattern without rounding it, so they do not
# consult the control word either -- the same sixteen pure moves 0acd1e32e5
# resolved through QEMU's call graph and left OUT of the x87 control-word read
# set on purpose.  LLVM attaches the use anyway, uniformly across the x87
# tables.  The tracer's set is the derived one.
x86_64 SR-rd-missing ffree +REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing ffreep +REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fld +REG_FPCW
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fld1 +REG_FPCW
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fldt +REG_FPCW
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fldz +REG_FPCW
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fst +REG_FPCW
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fst +REG_FPCW,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fstp +REG_FPCW
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fstp +REG_FPCW,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fstpt +REG_FPCW
x86_64 SR-rd-missing fxch +REG_FPCW

# --- X-SYSCALLFLAGS: SYSCALL reads RFLAGS because saving it into R11 is half of what the instruction does.
#     1 signature(s), 7014 encodings at this tip.
#
# SYSCALL is defined as `RCX <- RIP; R11 <- RFLAGS` (Intel SDM Vol. 2B,
# SYSCALL), so the flag register is a true source: the instruction copies it.
# QEMU states both writes and the read in helper_syscall()
# (target/i386/tcg/user/seg_helper.c), and the read reaches the wire's ordered
# list through the CP-H usage table's `eflags` and `cc_*` members.
#
# LLVM's MCInstrDesc carries a fixed Defs/Uses pair for the SYSCALL opcode and
# names no EFLAGS use on it at all -- the same structural silence the implicit
# family below meets, but about the FLAG register rather than a GPR, which is
# why it is its own row and not folded into that class.
#
# This signature is NEW at this tip and it is a repair, not a regression: the
# generated usage table had been carrying helper_syscall's footprint from
# before `ccfaf4fdc5` rewrote the helper, so the wire published nothing for
# nine members it depends on.  Restoring them made the tracer's read set a
# superset of the reference's, which is the direction this file exists to
# record.
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom syscall +REG_FLAGS

# --- X-IMPLICITGPR: the implicit GPR operands of the monitor / wait / key family.
#     16 signature(s), 28790 encodings at this tip.
#
# MONITORX, MWAITX, PCONFIG, RDPKRU, WRPKRU, TPAUSE and UMWAIT take implicit
# operands in fixed GPRs, and which of those the instruction READS depends on
# the leaf the register selects.  QEMU implements the leaf it models and reads
# what that leaf needs; LLVM's MCInstrDesc names the whole implicit set for the
# opcode.  The residue is the difference between a modelled leaf and a named
# one, and the tracer's is the read that happens.
x86_64 SR-rd-missing monitorx +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing mwaitx +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing pconfig +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rdpkru +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing tpause +REG_GPR#

# --- X-SSP: the shadow-stack pointer, which the reference names as a GPR or not at all.
#     11 signature(s), 5134 encodings at this tip.
#
# RDSSPD / RDSSPQ read SSP into a GPR; with CET off they are NOPs, which is why
# LLVM models the destination as SURVIVING and therefore read, while the
# tracer names the architectural source.  The remaining SSP rows are the CET
# instructions whose shadow-stack access the encoding does not name.  This is
# the `SR-` face of family (g) in the FR block above.

x86_64 SR-rd-missing rdsspd +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rdsspd +REG_SP
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rdsspq +REG_FP_REG
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rdsspq +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-missing rdsspq +REG_SP

x86_64 SR-rd-phantom saveprevssp +REG_SSP
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom setssbsy +REG_SSP,REG_SYS

# --- X-ADDRONLY: a hint whose memory operand is an ADDRESS the instruction computes and does not dereference.
#     6 signature(s), 9622 encodings at this tip.
#
# CLDEMOTE, PREFETCH* and the multi-byte NOP r/m all carry a ModRM memory
# operand whose EFFECTIVE ADDRESS is formed -- that is what the base register
# is for -- and whose contents are never touched.  QEMU forms the address, so
# the base register reaches the read list.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc models the
# operand as ignored and names no use, which is true of the DATA and false of
# the ADDRESS.
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom cldemote +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom nopl +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom nopq +REG_FP_REG
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom nopq +REG_GPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom nopq +REG_SP
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom nopw +REG_GPR#

# --- X-X87STATE: the x87 status word and the stack slot ST(0) resolves to, neither of which the reference names.
#     10 signature(s), 9712 encodings at this tip.
#
# The x87 status word carries the condition codes AND the stack top, so an
# instruction that sets C0-C3 read-modify-writes it and an instruction that
# names ST(i) must read the top to resolve i to a physical slot.  QEMU does
# both.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc names the architectural ST register and no status
# word at all.  STMXCSR and VSTMXCSR are the same silence from the other end:
# they STORE MXCSR, so reading it is the whole instruction, and LLVM still
# carries no use for it.
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom fcoml +REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom fcoms +REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom ficoml +REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom ficoms +REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom fistl +REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom fists +REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom fstl +REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom fsts +REG_FCSR,REG_FPR#
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom stmxcsr +REG_FCSR
x86_64 SR-rd-phantom vstmxcsr +REG_FCSR

# --- A-FPEN: the FP / SIMD / SVE / SME EXECUTION-ENABLE gate, which the reference models on no instruction.
#     1062 signature(s), 710456 encodings at this tip.
#
# Every FP, AdvSIMD, SVE and SME instruction runs an architectural enable check
# before it does anything -- CheckFPAdvSIMDEnabled, CheckSVEEnabled,
# CheckSMEEnabled (Arm ARM DDI 0487) -- and that check READS CPACR_EL1.FPEN and
# the CPTR_EL{2,3} traps.  QEMU states the read at translation time
# (fp_access_check() / sve_access_check() in target/arm/tcg/translate-a64.c),
# so it reaches the wire's ordered read list on the whole FP population.
# REG_SYSFPEN exists because that population is every FP instruction in the
# ISA: folded into the residual REG_SYS, one TTBR write would order all of them
# behind it.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc models no enable check on any instruction, so
# this is a phantom by construction and not a disagreement about dataflow.
#
# Where the qualifier names REG_VEC# beside the gate, the extra register is the
# instruction's own DESTINATION, read for the same reason the RVV block above
# gives: the predicated and merging forms leave the inactive elements of Zd
# standing, so Zd is a source.  Where it names REG_FCSR the register is FPSR,
# whose QC bit these forms make STICKY -- read-modify-written, not written --
# and where it names REG_FPCW it is FPCR, the control word every FP instruction
# reads.
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom abs +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom adclb +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom adclt +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom add +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addha +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addhn +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addhn2 +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addhnb +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addhnb +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VEC#
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addhnt +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addp +REG_SYSFPEN
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addp +REG_SYSFPEN,REG_VEC#
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addpl +REG_SYSFPEN

# --- A-GCS: the guarded control stack pointer, which the reference names on nothing.
#     10 signature(s), 51 encodings at this tip.
#
# The GCS instructions push to, pop from and switch the guarded control stack,
# all through GCSPR_ELx, and none of them names it in the encoding.  LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc has no operand for it.  REG_SSP is its own dependency population
# -- neither the data stack pointer nor the link register -- so the read is
# recorded there.
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom gcspopcx +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom gcspopm +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom gcspopx +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom gcspushm +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom gcspushx +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom gcsss1 +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom gcsss2 +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom sys +REG_SSP
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom sysl +REG_SSP

# --- A-SYSSEL: the system register an (op0, op1, CRn, CRm, op2) tuple selects, which LLVM models as an immediate.
#     27 signature(s), 19749 encodings at this tip.
#
# MRS / MSR / MRRS / MSRR / SYS / SYSL address a system register by an ENCODED
# TUPLE, and LLVM's MCInstrDesc carries that tuple as immediates: there is no
# register operand for the reference to name.  The tracer names the register,
# in the dependence-behaviour group cap_aarch64_sysreg_class puts it in, so
# MRS produces one signature per group where it produced one in total.  The
# `SR-rd-missing sysl +REG_GPR#` companion is the same operand read the other
# way: LLVM names the destination GPR of SYSL as a USE.
#
# `msr +REG_FPCW` and `msrr +REG_FPCW` are the WRITE half, new at this tip
# because FPCR moved off REG_FCSR (the control word is not the status word);
# LLVM models no system register write on MSR either.
aarch64 SR-rd-missing sysl +REG_GPR#
aarch64 SR-rd-missing tlbi +REG_GPR#
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_FCSR
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_FPCW
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYS
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSCACHE
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSDBG
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSEXC
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSID
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSMMU
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSPERF
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrrs +REG_SYSTIMER
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_FCSR
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_FPCW
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYS
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSCACHE
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSDBG
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSEXC
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSID
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSMMU
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSPERF
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_SYSTIMER
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom mrs +REG_VCTRL
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom sysl +REG_SYS
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom sysl +REG_SYSCACHE
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom sysl +REG_SYSDBG
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom sysl +REG_SYSMMU

# --- A-MTE: the MTE tag-control registers, read implicitly.
#     7 signature(s), 3357 encodings at this tip.
#
# IRG, ADDG, SUBG, LDGM, STGM and STZGM take their behaviour from GCR_EL1 (the
# tag-exclusion mask) and GMID_EL1 (the tag-granule size), and ST64BV0 from
# ACCDATA_EL1.  All are implicit reads the encoding does not name and LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc does not model.
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom addg +REG_SYS
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom irg +REG_SYS
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom ldgm +REG_SYSID
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom st64bv0 +REG_SYS
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom stgm +REG_SYSID
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom stzgm +REG_SYSID
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom subg +REG_SYS

# --- A-ERET: exception return reads the state exception entry wrote.
#     5 signature(s), 5 encodings at this tip.
#
# ERET, ERETAA and ERETAB restore PC and PSTATE from ELR_ELx and SPSR_ELx, so
# both are true sources; the authenticated forms additionally read the stack
# pointer as the PAC modifier, which is what the wire records and what LLVM
# spells as a read of the link register instead.  LLVM models no exception
# state on any of the three.
aarch64 SR-rd-missing eretaa +REG_LR
aarch64 SR-rd-missing eretab +REG_LR
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom eret +REG_SYSEXC
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom eretaa +REG_SYSEXC
aarch64 SR-rd-phantom eretab +REG_SYSEXC

# --- A-SATQC: the saturating forms read FPSR.QC because QC is STICKY, and LLVM names FPCR instead.
#     2 signature(s), 192 encodings at this tip.
#
# SQDMULH and SQRDMULH set FPSR.QC on saturation, and QC is a STICKY bit: the
# instruction must read the accumulated value to OR into it, so FPSR is
# read-modify-written and the read is real.  LLVM instead attaches a use of
# FPCR to these opcodes -- the control word, which a fixed-point saturating
# multiply does not consult -- so the two sides name two different registers
# and neither names the other's.  The wire is right on both counts, and the
# disagreement is only VISIBLE now because FPCR and FPSR stopped sharing an id
# at this tip.
aarch64 SR-rd-missing sqdmulh +REG_FPCW
aarch64 SR-rd-missing sqrdmulh +REG_FPCW

# --- A-MISC: the remaining single-signature reference gaps.
#     2 signature(s), 6232 encodings at this tip.
#
# `bc.nv` is the never-taken consistent branch: LLVM names a condition-flag
# read for the whole BC.cond family, and QEMU reads nothing because the NV
# condition is resolved at translation time.  `tlbi` with a register argument
# LLVM names as a GPR use on the forms that take none.  `zero za...` LLVM names
# as a read of the ZA array it only writes.  Each is one signature and each is
# the reference over-naming an operand its per-opcode description carries.
aarch64 SR-rd-missing bc.nv +REG_FLAGS
aarch64 SR-rd-missing zero +REG_MATRIX

# --- R-HINT: an RV128 mnemonic decoded on RV64, where the encoding is a HINT that reads nothing.
#     8 signature(s), 12335 encodings at this tip.
#
# C.SLLI64 / C.SRLI64 / C.SRAI64 are the RV128 spellings of the compressed
# shifts with shamt == 0.  On RV64 that encoding is not those instructions:
# the unprivileged spec (Ch. 16, "C" extension) makes shamt == 0 a HINT, and
# QEMU implements the hint -- nothing is read and nothing is written.  LLVM's
# RV64 decoder still resolves the RV128 opcode and names its register operand,
# so the reference names a read the architecture does not perform on this
# target.  The wire is right.
riscv64 SR-rd-missing c.slli64 +REG_FP_REG
riscv64 SR-rd-missing c.slli64 +REG_GPR#
riscv64 SR-rd-missing c.slli64 +REG_LR
riscv64 SR-rd-missing c.slli64 +REG_SP
riscv64 SR-rd-missing c.srai64 +REG_FP_REG
riscv64 SR-rd-missing c.srai64 +REG_GPR#
riscv64 SR-rd-missing c.srli64 +REG_FP_REG
riscv64 SR-rd-missing c.srli64 +REG_GPR#

# --- R-ZIMOP: Zimop may-be-operations write zero and read nothing.
#     174 signature(s), 856 encodings at this tip.
#
# `mop.r.N rd, rs1` and `mop.rr.N rd, rs1, rs2` are Zimop's "may be operations":
# when no extension has claimed the encoding they are defined to write ZERO to
# rd, and their source registers are architecturally unread.  QEMU implements
# exactly that.  LLVM's MCInstrDesc names the source operands because the
# encoding HAS them, which is a statement about the syntax and not about the
# dataflow.  The wire is right.

riscv64 SR-rd-missing mop.r.0 +REG_LR
riscv64 SR-rd-missing mop.r.0 +REG_SP

# --- R-PAIR: AMOCAS.Q's even-odd register PAIR, of which LLVM names only the even half.
#     6 signature(s), 121 encodings at this tip.
#
# Zacas AMOCAS.Q on RV64 compares and swaps 128 bits, so its compare value and
# its swap value are each an EVEN-ODD REGISTER PAIR -- rd/rd+1 and rs2/rs2+1 --
# and QEMU reads both halves.  LLVM's MCInst carries one operand for the pair,
# spelled with the even register, so the odd half is a read it structurally
# cannot name.  The wire is right, and the pair is the whole difference.
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom amocas.q +REG_GPR#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom amocas.q +REG_GPR#,REG_LR
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom amocas.q +REG_LR
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom amocas.q.aq +REG_GPR#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom amocas.q.aqrl +REG_GPR#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom amocas.q.rl +REG_GPR#

# --- R-LPAD: Zicfilp's landing-pad label check reads x7.
#     1 signature(s), 96 encodings at this tip.
#
# `lpad` compares the encoded label against the low bits of x7 and traps on a
# mismatch, so x7 is a true source and the landing-pad state is read beside it.
# LLVM models the label as an immediate and names no register at all.
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom lpad +REG_GPR#,REG_SYS

# --- R-VTAIL: RVV reads its own destination under a policy the ENCODING does not carry.
#     569 signature(s), 30953 encodings at this tip.
#
# Under the tail-undisturbed and mask-undisturbed policies the elements of vd
# before vstart, the elements a mask bit turns off, and the elements past vl
# all RETAIN THE VALUE vd ALREADY HELD ("V" spec v1.0, sec. 5.3 and 5.4).  On
# those instructions vd is a genuine source.
#
# WHICH POLICY IS IN FORCE IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE INSTRUCTION.  It is vtype's
# vta/vma, set by a vset*vli that may be arbitrarily far away, so the encoding
# this row describes does not determine it and NO static description of that
# encoding can.  That is the whole of the argument, and it is the reason the
# row is about a static model rather than about a run: for one encoding there
# are two architecturally-defined behaviours, one of which reads vd, and the
# encoding does not say which.  A description that omits vd is therefore wrong
# under a state the architecture permits, while one that carries it is a
# superset -- an edge that is sometimes inert, never an edge that is missing.
# A missing dependency is the error direction this project treats as a defect;
# an inert one costs precision.
#
# WHAT WAS PREVIOUSLY WRITTEN HERE WAS FALSE and is recorded so the same
# reasoning is not rebuilt: this row used to justify itself by calling
# tail/mask-undisturbed "the reset state of vtype and the one QEMU's helpers
# implement unconditionally".  Neither is true.  riscv_cpu_reset_hold() sets
# env->vill, not a tu/mu vtype; and target/riscv/vector_helper.c implements
# BOTH policies -- vext_set_elems_1s() is called exactly when vta/vma is set,
# which is the agnostic case overwriting the elements the old text claimed were
# always preserved.  The rows this class emits do not change: they never rested
# on that claim, only on the argument above.
#
# LLVM's MCInstrDesc carries a tied operand only on the forms whose ASSEMBLY
# repeats the register, so on the rest its read set is short by exactly vd --
# it is a per-encoding description facing the same missing fact, and it
# resolves it the other way.  This is the same disagreement MIPS lwl/lwr
# already carries in this file, and the fields side is again the correct
# one.
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom vaadd.vv +REG_FCSR,REG_VEC#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom vaadd.vx +REG_FCSR,REG_VEC#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom vaaddu.vv +REG_FCSR,REG_VEC#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom vaaddu.vx +REG_FCSR,REG_VEC#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom vadc.vim +REG_VEC#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom vadc.vvm +REG_VEC#
riscv64 SR-rd-phantom vadc.vxm +REG_VEC#

# --- R-CSRIMM: the CSR a number selects, which LLVM models as an IMMEDIATE.
#     301 signature(s), 22893 encodings at this tip.
#
# A CSR instruction addresses its register by a 12-bit NUMBER, and LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc carries that number as an immediate operand: there is no register
# operand for the reference to name, whatever the number selects.  The tracer
# names the register, in the behaviour class the CSR table (371 entries) puts
# it in -- REG_FCSR for the FP control-and-status file, REG_VCTRL for the
# vector configuration, REG_SSP for Zicfiss ssp, REG_SYS for the long tail.
# This is the boundary file's own `riscv64 SR-rd-phantom * +REG_SYS` row seen
# per-family at this layer.  R16: the dependency is the ISA's, and a reference
# that does not model the register cannot refute it.

riscv64 SR-rd-phantom wfi +REG_SYS

# --- M-LSA: the four bytes are TWO INSTRUCTIONS, and on this model QEMU runs the other one -- FINDING 70-A, RESOLVED.
#     1 signature(s), 89 encodings at this tip.
#
# THE SURPLUS IS ONE REGISTER, $a0, AND IT IS QEMU'S OWN STATEMENT.  SPECIAL
# function 0x05 is decoded in three different places depending on the model:
# rel6.decode:21 and msa.decode:51 both spell it LSA, and a model that is
# NEITHER R6 nor MSA-bearing reaches translate.c:17241 `case OPC_PMON` -- the
# PMON entry point, marked "unofficial" at translate.c:253, which emits
# gen_helper_pmon().  That helper's body reads env->active_tc.gpr[4] on three
# of its arms (op_helper.c:238, :247, :253).  REG_GPR4 is that read.  The
# tracer publishes what the emulator executes.
#
# WHY THE REFERENCE DISAGREES, AND WHY THAT IS NOT A TRACER FINDING.  LLVM MC
# decodes the word as `lsa` because `lsa` is what SPECIAL-0x05 is in MIPS32R6,
# and it is not asked which model the corpus was captured on.  The two sides
# are describing DIFFERENT INSTRUCTIONS at the same address, which this gate
# already has a name for -- a JOIN FAILURE, "not a tracer finding", and a row
# "no dataflow change can close" (see the join commentary in
# isax_srcenc_gate.sh).  The join test does not catch this one because it
# compares the two decoders' MNEMONICS and Capstone is the defective side:
# Capstone 6.0 prints `lsa` in MIPS32 and MIPS32R2 mode too, where SPECIAL-0x05
# is unallocated (exec122/CAPSTONE_PROBE.txt, reported upstream and not worked
# around).  With both decoders saying `lsa` the join succeeds and the register
# difference is scored as dataflow.
#
# THE DIRECTION IS DECIDED BY CONSULTING THE MODEL, NOT BY REFUSING THE ROW.
# The row was left NOT ALLOWLISTED for four passes on the reasoning that "an
# allowlist row asserts the tracer is right" -- written before anyone had
# checked whether it is.  It is: op_helper.c:233 is the witness.  Refusing the
# row instead would assert a permanent tracer defect that no dataflow change
# can ever close, and it would state a per-model difference as tracer scope,
# which the all-MIPS-models ruling forbids.  The difference is a QEMU-modelling
# fact and it is recorded here as one, with the model named.
#
# SELF-RETIRING FROM EITHER END.  A Capstone that stops decoding SPECIAL-0x05
# in a pre-R6 mode makes the join fail on the mnemonic and this row goes dead;
# so does a corpus captured on an R6 or MSA-bearing model, where the word IS
# `lsa` and gpr[4] is never read.  Both are the dead-allowlist-rule detector's
# business, and it is watching this row.
#
# THE ADJUDICATION LIVES HERE AND NOT IN THE ALLOWLIST (FINDING 77-B).  It was
# written into the generated block by hand in 384a50e331, which edited the two
# allowlist files and not this one -- so the first regeneration threw the whole
# class back to DEFECT and both mipsel arms went red again, which is how it was
# found.  A class's verdict is the generator's; a hand-edit inside the block is
# a verdict with a deletion date.
mipsel SR-rd-phantom lsa +REG_GPR#

# --- M-MERGE: the unaligned loads merge into their destination, so the destination is a source.
#     8 signature(s), 8064 encodings at this tip.
#
# LWL / LWR / LWLE / LWRE write only the bytes their address selects and leave
# the rest of $rt standing, so the old $rt is a true source.  LLVM's
# MCInstrDesc agrees and its MCInst drops the tie, which is the same shape the
# boundary block for lwl/lwr in this file already carries.
mipsel SR-rd-phantom lwl +REG_GPR#
mipsel SR-rd-phantom lwle +REG_GPR#,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom lwle +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom lwr +REG_GPR#
mipsel SR-rd-phantom lwre +REG_GPR#,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom lwre +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom swle +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom swre +REG_SYSEXC

# --- M-MT: the MT ASE far operand, reported in the file the (u, sel) pair selects.
#     13 signature(s), 8448 encodings at this tip.
#
# MFTR / MTTR move between thread contexts and the (u, sel) pair chooses WHICH
# FILE the far operand lives in -- GPR, FPR, accumulator, CP0, the DSP control
# word.  The tracer reports the register in the file the pair selects; LLVM
# models the pair as immediates and names the GPR encoding regardless.  The
# paired phantom and missing entries are ONE operand reclassified, not two
# findings, exactly as the boundary block for this family already states.
mipsel SR-rd-missing mftr +REG_GPR#
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_ACC#,REG_ACCHI#,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_COPROC0,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_FCSR,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_FLAGS,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_FPR#,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_SYS,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_SYSDBG,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSID
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mftr +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSTIMER
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mttr +REG_SYSEXC

# --- M-CP0: the CP0 register an instruction owns, in the class the split put it in.
#     20 signature(s), 3299 encodings at this tip.
#
# The TLB maintenance instructions, the exception returns and the COP0 moves
# name their coprocessor-0 registers through a (rd, sel) pair or implicitly;
# QEMU's helpers name them one at a time and the tracer records the class --
# REG_SYSMMU for the TLB file, REG_SYSEXC for Status/Cause/EPC, REG_SYSDBG for
# the EJTAG file, REG_SYSID for the read-only identification registers.  LLVM's
# MIPS tables model no CP0 register on any of these forms.
mipsel SR-rd-missing ctcmsa +REG_COPROC0
mipsel SR-rd-missing ctcmsa +REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom cfcmsa +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom ctcmsa +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom deret +REG_SYSDBG,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom dvpe +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom eret +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom evpe +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mfc0 +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mfc2 +REG_FPR#,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mtc0 +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom mtc2 +REG_FPR#,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom sdbbp +REG_SYSDBG,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom tlbinv +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom tlbinvf +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom tlbp +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom tlbr +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom tlbwi +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom tlbwr +REG_SYSEXC,REG_SYSMMU
mipsel SR-rd-phantom wait +REG_SYSEXC

# --- M-FCSR: FCR31 / MSACSR is read-modify-written by every COP1 and MSA arithmetic instruction.
#     200 signature(s), 39384 encodings at this tip.
#
# QEMU's update_fcr31() read-modify-writes the word on every call -- it
# SET_FP_CAUSEs into it and reads GET_FP_ENABLE out of it -- and update_msacsr()
# is the same shape, so the register is a true source of the instruction that
# updates it.  LLVM's MIPS tables model no FP status register at all.  This is
# the fields-layer face of the same fact the FR block above already carries;
# what is new is the SIGNATURE, not the argument.
mipsel SR-rd-phantom add.d +REG_FCSR
mipsel SR-rd-phantom add.ps +REG_FCSR,REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom add.s +REG_FCSR
mipsel SR-rd-phantom addr.ps +REG_FCSR,REG_SYSEXC

# --- M-EXC: the footprint of the exception an instruction RAISES.
#     622 signature(s), 191639 encodings at this tip.
#
# R7.6: syscall, break, the twelve conditional traps, the overflow-checking
# arithmetic and the DSP forms whose availability is gated all reach QEMU's
# exception path, which read-modify-writes Status and Cause and writes EPC
# (tlb_helper.c).  The tracer records that footprint; LLVM MC models no
# exception entry on any instruction, so it is a phantom on both the read and
# the write because the model is a read-modify-write.  Again the fields-layer
# face of the FR block above, under the `SR-` key.
mipsel SR-rd-phantom absq_s.ph +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom absq_s.qb +REG_SYSEXC
mipsel SR-rd-phantom absq_s.w +REG_SYSEXC
